**102. [src/content/guides/pl/what-is-nostr.mdx:15] Polish guide is missing three sections that were later added to the English source, including the paragraph that actually defines Nostr.**

- Evidence: PL file jumps from the import block straight to "### Problem (1 minuta czytania)" (line 15). EN has, before that section: "**Nostr — short for Notes and Other Stuff Transmitted by Relays — is an open protocol for social media, not an app.**" plus the fiatjaf/NIPs paragraph (EN lines 21-23); EN also has "## How Nostr Actually Works" (Event/Kind/Signature/NIP/Web of trust + zap paragraph, EN lines 175-185) and "### Coming From Somewhere Else?" with 4 comparison links (EN lines 187-195). None exist in PL. The PL closing line 175 also links only FAQ, while EN line 207 links FAQ, glossary and resources. Git confirms: EN was expanded in commit 86cd625 ("define Nostr"); the PL file predates it, as do all other locales.
- Fix: Translate and insert: (1) EN lines 21-23 right after the import block; (2) EN lines 175-195 ("How Nostr Actually Works" + "Coming From Somewhere Else?") before "## Sprawdź swoją wiedzę"; (3) extend the footer to match EN line 207. Link rules: guide links get the /pl prefix (/pl/guides/keys-and-security, /pl/guides/nip05-identity, /pl/guides/zaps-and-lightning, /pl/guides/finding-community, /pl/guides/protocol-comparison, /pl/guides/faq); glossary is localized, use /pl/glossary; /nostr-vs-twitter, /nostr-vs-mastodon, /nostr-vs-bluesky and /resources are top-level unlocalized pages, keep them unprefixed. Keep terms npub/nsec/relay/NIP/zap/sats untranslated per docs/internal/CONTENT_TRANSLATION.md.
**103. [src/content/guides/zh/faq.mdx:452] Zap FAQ adds a 5-step list with a hardcoded fiat conversion "1000 聪 = ~$0.30" that is not in the EN source and is wrong today (~$0.78 at BTC ≈ $78k).**

- Evidence: Lines 452–456: "1. 你创建了好的内容 → 一条帖子、线程或回复 ... 3. 他们发送聪 → 少量比特币（1000 聪 = ~$0.30）... 5. 每个人都看到 → 闪电打赏是公开的，创造社交证明". EN "How zaps work" is 4 steps with no price: "1. Someone enjoys your post 2. They \"zap\" you (send Bitcoin) 3. You receive satoshis (tiny Bitcoin amounts) 4. Instant, low-fee, global". BTC traded around $78,300 on 2026-09-01, so the implied $30k rate is stale by ~2.6x.
- Fix: Replace the 5-step list with a translation of the current EN 4 steps and drop the fiat conversion entirely: "1. 有人喜欢你的帖子 2. 他们'打赏'你（发送比特币）3. 你收到聪（极小额的比特币）4. 即时、低费用、全球可用".
**104. [src/content/guides/zh/finding-community.mdx:444] Inverted conditional turns the DM-safety prevention tip into nonsense: 'if you don't mind' instead of 'if you don't mean it'.**

- Evidence: Line 444, under '预防：' in the DM etiquette section: '如果你不介意，不要发布你的私信是开放的' — EN source (line 447): 'Don't post your DMs are open if you don't mean it'. The zh reads 'If you don't mind, don't post that your DMs are open', which reverses the condition and leaves the reader with no usable advice in an anti-harassment context.
- Fix: Replace with: '如果你并不真的欢迎陌生人私信，就不要宣称"私信开放"'.
**105. [src/content/guides/zh/protocol-comparison.mdx:259] "Pseudonymous by default" translated as "anonymous by default", overstating Nostr's privacy guarantees.**

- Evidence: "✅ 默认即可匿名（假名）使用" (Privacy Considerations, Nostr list, line 259). EN source says "✅ Pseudonymous by default" and deliberately avoids the word anonymous; the zh privacy-security guide itself says "你的 npub 是假名的，不是匿名的", which this line contradicts.
- Fix: Replace "默认即可匿名（假名）使用" with "默认使用假名（无需实名）". Do not use 匿名 here; the whole EN paragraph's point is that Nostr gives pseudonymity, not anonymity.
**106. [src/content/guides/zh/what-is-nostr.mdx:20] Whole sections of the EN source are missing from the zh guide: the opening definition paragraph, the entire 'How Nostr Actually Works' section, and the 'Coming From Somewhere Else?' comparison section.**

- Evidence: zh content starts directly at '### 问题所在（1 分钟阅读）' (line 20) with no equivalent of EN lines 21-23 ('**Nostr — short for Notes and Other Stuff Transmitted by Relays — is an open protocol for social media, not an app.** ... published anonymously by a developer known as fiatjaf in 2020...'). After the quiz, zh ends at line 180 with only a FAQ link, while EN lines 175-195 contain '## How Nostr Actually Works' (Event, Kind, Signature, NIP, Web of trust, zaps paragraph) and '### Coming From Somewhere Else?' (links to /nostr-vs-twitter, /nostr-vs-mastodon, /nostr-vs-bluesky, /guides/protocol-comparison). EN footer also links the glossary and /resources; zh has only FAQ.
- Fix: Translate and append the three missing blocks to zh/what-is-nostr.mdx: (1) the bold intro definition paragraph plus the fiatjaf/2020/NIPs sentence, placed before '### 问题所在'; (2) '## How Nostr Actually Works' with the five bullet definitions (keep terms 事件/kind/签名/NIP/信任网络, keep NIP numbers and kind integers verbatim) and the zap paragraph using 打赏（zap）; (3) '### Coming From Somewhere Else?' with the three comparison links (those pages are English-only, so keep unprefixed URLs) and a /zh/guides/protocol-comparison link. Internal guide links inside these sections must use the /zh/ prefix (/zh/guides/keys-and-security, /zh/guides/nip05-identity, /zh/guides/zaps-and-lightning, /zh/guides/finding-community). Keep the footer glossary link OUT (glossary ships only en/pl/es/de) but add the /resources link or consciously note its omission. Note for the caller: pl, es and de have the identical drift (the sections exist only in EN), so this is an EN-update-not-propagated problem across all locales, not a zh-only defect.
**107. [src/data/glossary/en.ts:97] DM definition presents deprecated NIP-04 as a current, equal option for encrypted private messaging.**

- Evidence: "Direct Message. Encrypted private messages between Nostr users (NIP-04 or NIP-17)." (en.ts line 97, 'dm' entry; mirrored verbatim in pl.ts:97, es.ts:97, de.ts:97). The NIPs repo marks NIP-04 "unrecommended: deprecated in favor of NIP-17" and NIP-04 itself warns it "leaks metadata in the events" (verified 2026-09-01 at raw.githubusercontent.com/nostr-protocol/nips/master/04.md). The site's own guide linked from this term (TERM_GUIDES dm -> 'nip17-private-messages') and en.json ("Modern clients like Damus, Amethyst, and others have adopted NIP-17 for private messaging") already teach NIP-17 only, so the glossary contradicts the rest of the site.
- Fix: Rewrite the 'dm' definition in all four files. EN: 'Direct Message. Encrypted private messages between Nostr users. Modern clients use NIP-17; the older NIP-04 format is deprecated because it reveals who is messaging whom.' PL: 'Direct Message — wiadomość bezpośrednia. Szyfrowane prywatne wiadomości między użytkownikami Nostr. Nowoczesne klienty używają NIP-17; starszy NIP-04 jest wycofany, bo ujawnia, kto z kim pisze.' ES: 'Mensaje directo (Direct Message). Mensajes privados cifrados entre usuarios de Nostr. Los clientes modernos usan NIP-17; el antiguo NIP-04 está obsoleto porque revela quién habla con quién.' DE: 'Direct Message — Direktnachricht. Verschlüsselte private Nachrichten zwischen Nostr-Nutzern. Moderne Clients nutzen NIP-17; das ältere NIP-04 gilt als veraltet, weil es verrät, wer mit wem schreibt.'
**108. [src/i18n/locales/en.json:2588] The Current client is dead but is still listed in the comparison table and client recommender.**

- Evidence: clientComparisonTable.clients.current.description: "iOS client with excellent Nostr Wallet Connect integration." (en.json line 2588). Verified 2026-09-01: last commit to starbackr-com/current is 2023-09-17; App Store link https://apps.apple.com/app/current-nostr-client/id1668517032 returns 404 and iTunes lookup for id 1668517032 returns resultCount:0 (app removed); app.getcurrent.io does not respond. The link is hardcoded in src/components/interactive/ClientComparisonTable.tsx:77 and src/components/interactive/ClientRecommender.tsx:177, so the 'Get Current' button sends beginners to a 404.
- Fix: Remove the Current entry: delete clientComparisonTable.clients.current.* keys from all locale files, and drop the client object plus CLIENT_URLS.current from ClientComparisonTable.tsx and the corresponding entry in ClientRecommender.tsx (retune the iOS+wallet recommendation path to Primal, which has a built-in wallet, or Damus).
**109. [src/i18n/locales/en.json:2632] Plebstr no longer exists — its Play Store link now installs Openvibe, a multi-network client.**

- Evidence: clientComparisonTable.clients.plebstr.description: "A simple Android client for beginners." (en.json line 2632). Verified 2026-09-01: the linked package https://play.google.com/store/apps/details?id=com.plebstr.client (ClientComparisonTable.tsx:80) now shows "Openvibe – Mastodon & Bluesky"; the Plebstr team (Tweetoshi s.r.o.) rebranded the app into Openvibe, a combined Nostr/Bluesky/Mastodon/Threads client. A beginner tapping 'Get Plebstr' installs an app with a different name and different onboarding.
- Fix: Remove the Plebstr entry (keys clientComparisonTable.clients.plebstr.* in all locales, the client object and CLIENT_URLS.plebstr in ClientComparisonTable.tsx, and any ClientRecommender mapping). If a beginner-Android slot is wanted, Amethyst already covers Android; alternatively list Primal for Android as the easy option.
**110. [src/i18n/locales/en.json:2603] Habla, the table's long-form pick for writers, links to a site that is down (404).**

- Evidence: clientComparisonTable.clients.habla.description: "Long-form content focused client with newsletter support." (en.json line 2603). Verified 2026-09-01: https://habla.news/ and https://habla.news/read both return HTTP 404 with body "The deployment could not be found on Vercel. DEPLOYMENT_NOT_FOUND"; the verbiricha/habla.news repo's last commit is 2025-03-26. The link is at ClientComparisonTable.tsx:78. This is the entry aimed squarely at the site's creator/writer audience.
- Fix: Replace Habla with a live long-form client verified at ship time (e.g. YakiHonne or Highlighter — check the URL responds before committing), or remove the entry until habla.news is back. Also drop the unverifiable "newsletter support" claim from the replacement description.
**111. [src/i18n/locales/en.json:766] Quiz tells creators to use BlueWallet as a self-custodial Lightning wallet, but BlueWallet dropped Lightning in 2023.**

- Evidence: guides.zapsAndLightning.quiz.questions[2].explanation (id wallet-setup): "To receive zaps, you need a Lightning wallet (custodial like Wallet of Satoshi, or self-custodial like Phoenix/BlueWallet) linked to your Nostr profile via a Lightning Address." (en.json line 766). BlueWallet sunset its LNDHub Lightning service on 2023-04-30 (bluewallet.io/sunsetting-lndhub); the app now only supports connecting your own self-hosted LNDHub. A beginner who installs BlueWallet cannot receive zaps and gets no Lightning Address.
- Fix: Rewrite the explanation with wallets that work in Sept 2026, e.g.: "To receive zaps, you need a Lightning wallet that gives you a Lightning Address — for example Primal's built-in wallet or Coinos (easy, custodial) or Zeus (self-custodial) — linked to your Nostr profile." Remove BlueWallet; see the separate finding about Wallet of Satoshi before keeping it as the custodial example.
**112. [src/i18n/locales/en.json:353] Quiz's marked-correct answer claims many clients import Twitter followers — that feature effectively no longer exists.**

- Evidence: guides.findingCommunity.quiz.questions[2] (id twitter-import): correct option label "Yes, many clients let you import Twitter followers who have Nostr accounts" (en.json line 353) and explanation "Many Nostr clients offer Twitter import features...". These features (Snort/nostr.directory sign-in-with-Twitter era, 2023) broke after X locked down its API; no current major client (Damus, Amethyst, Primal, Snort) offers Twitter import in 2026, and even nostr.how now suggests manual search tricks instead. The quiz teaches new users to hunt for a feature they won't find.
- Fix: Rewrite the question so the correct answer reflects reality, e.g. correct option: "Only if they've joined Nostr and shared their npub — there's no automatic import", with an explanation pointing to what actually works: follow packs / starter packs, searching people by name or NIP-05, and asking friends directly for their npub.
**113. [src/i18n/locales/es.json:12] Most es quiz question sets are an older, entirely different quiz than the current English source, not translations of it**

- Evidence: guides.whatIsNostr.quiz.questions[0].prompt is "¿Qué es Nostr?" (id 'protocol-vs-platform') where EN asks "What does NOSTR stand for?" (id 'nostr-acronym'). Script comparison of the extracted quiz content: 55 of 71 question ids and 41 of 71 correctIds differ from en.json, concentrated in guides.whatIsNostr, keysAndSecurity, findingCommunity[0-4], relaysDemystified[0-4], nip05Identity, zapsAndLightning, troubleshooting, multiClient, relayGuide, privacySecurity, nip17PrivateMessages and protocolComparison[0,3,4]. de.json and ar.json have 0/71 mismatches, proving the intended shape is a 1:1 translation of the current EN quizzes; es shares its stale set with pl (identical 41/55 counts). Content consequences include es teaching "5-15 relays" as the right count (guides.relaysDemystified.quiz.questions[2], guides.relayGuide.quiz.questions[1]) where current EN teaches 3-10, and missing the current EN security questions entirely (e.g. EN keysAndSecurity 'suspicious-client' with its NIP-46 signer answer).
- Fix: Re-translate the affected guide quizzes in es.json from the current en.json question sets, keeping EN question ids, option ids, option order and correctId, and translating only prompt/title/options/explanation (use de.json as the structural reference). Guides already current and needing no change: outboxModel (except the q4 explanation, separate finding), findingCommunity[5], relaysDemystified[5], keysAndSecurity structure differs throughout so redo all six. Same fix will be needed for pl.json.
**114. [src/i18n/locales/es.json:200] es security quiz marks using a signer extension with temporary permissions as a WRONG way to safely try a new client, contradicting the guide's own security teaching**

- Evidence: guides.keysAndSecurity.quiz.questions[2]: prompt "¿Cómo probar un cliente Nostr de forma segura?", options a: "Usar extensión con permisos temporales", b: "Introducir tu nsec real y probar", c: "Crear cuenta de prueba separada" with correctId "c". A reader who picks the signer-extension answer (a) is told they are wrong, while the current EN question ('suspicious-client') teaches exactly that: "Use a signer / hardware key or wait until the client supports delegated signing" is the correct answer.
- Fix: Replace this question with a faithful translation of the current EN 'suspicious-client' question (options paste/signer/temp, correct = signer), e.g. correct option: "Usar un signer / llave de hardware, o esperar a que el cliente soporte firma delegada". This disappears automatically if the whole keysAndSecurity quiz is re-translated per the main finding.
**115. [src/i18n/locales/pl.json:278] The keys-and-security zap question marks publishing your Lightning address as unsafe, directly contradicting the zaps guide quiz that teaches it as the correct first step to receive zaps.**

- Evidence: guides.keysAndSecurity.quiz.questions[5] (id "zaps", prompt "Jak bezpiecznie konfigurować zaps?") lists option c "Udostępnić adres Lightning publicznie" as a wrong answer, while guides.zapsAndLightning.quiz.questions[3] (id "receiving", prompt "Jak odebrać pierwszego zapsa?") marks "Udostępnić adres Lightning w profilu" (line 776) as the CORRECT answer. Receiving zaps requires a public Lightning address in kind:0 metadata; PL-only content, no such conflict exists in en.json.
- Fix: In guides.keysAndSecurity.quiz.questions[5], replace option c's label with a genuinely unsafe act mirroring the EN distractor, e.g. "Wysłać komuś seed phrase swojego portfela". Keep correctId "b".
**116. [src/i18n/locales/pl.json:204] The suspicious-client question penalizes using a signer/extension with temporary permissions, the very practice the site elsewhere teaches as best practice.**

- Evidence: guides.keysAndSecurity.quiz.questions[2] (id "suspicious-client", prompt "Jak bezpiecznie przetestować nowy klient Nostr?") marks option a "Użyć rozszerzenia z tymczasowymi uprawnieniami" as wrong (correctId "c", test account). EN's version of this question makes the signer route the CORRECT answer ("Use a signer / hardware key..."), and PL's own guides.privacySecurity.quiz.questions[2] explanation says signer apps are "znacznie bezpieczniejsze". A user picking the best-practice answer is told they are wrong.
- Fix: Replace option a with a clearly unsafe distractor, e.g. "Wkleić nsec głównego konta, jeśli klient wygląda na popularny" (option b already covers pasting nsec, so alternatively make BOTH a and c acceptable by rewriting the question to match EN: signer as correct, throwaway key as distractor).
**117. [src/i18n/locales/zh.json:827] Seven zh quizzes plus their guide descriptions are a stale question set unique to zh, not translations of the English source: troubleshooting, multiClient, relayGuide, privacySecurity, nip17PrivateMessages, protocolComparison, outboxModel.**

- Evidence: EN troubleshooting question ids are [empty-feed, posts-not-appearing, connection-issues, missing-history, dm-not-working, slow-performance]; zh has [missing-posts (line 827), follows-gone (849), cant-post, zaps-not-working, slow-loading, dm-fail]. Same pattern for multiClient (zh line 968), relayGuide (1087), privacySecurity (1206), nip17PrivateMessages (1347), protocolComparison (1466), outboxModel (1585). de/ar/hi track the current EN set, pl/es share a different old set, zh matches nothing. Guide descriptions diverge too, e.g. guides.troubleshooting.description: EN "Common issues and how to fix them. From empty feeds to relay problems." vs zh "解决 Nostr 上常见问题。找不到帖子？关注被重置？本指南帮你解决。" Two id collisions are hazardous: multiClient.same-identity has correctId "b" in EN but "a" in zh, and nip17PrivateMessages.migration has "b" in EN but "a" in zh — each internally consistent, but any cross-locale per-question logic or analytics keyed on question id would compare different questions with different answers.
- Fix: Re-translate the seven quiz blocks and the five guide descriptions (troubleshooting, multiClient, relayGuide, privacySecurity, nip17PrivateMessages, protocolComparison, outboxModel) from current en.json so question ids, options and correctIds match the source. This also removes the weaker claims only zh carries, e.g. outboxModel outbox-privacy (line 1657) presenting lesser-known relays as a privacy measure. Note pl/es have their own, different divergence — same fix applies there but is out of this locale's scope.

### medium
**118. [src/content/guides/ar/faq.mdx:752] Garbled mixed-script fragment "ت compromised" left inside the npub-guessing security answer.**

- Evidence: Line 752: "معرفة npub وحدها لا يمكن أن ت compromised حسابك." An orphaned Arabic prefix "ت" is glued to the untranslated English word "compromised"; under RTL rendering this displays as jumbled text mid-sentence in a security answer.
- Fix: Change to: "معرفة npub وحدها لا يمكن أن تعرّض حسابك للخطر."
**119. [src/content/guides/ar/faq.mdx:251] FAQ coins its own relay term "المرحلات" (about 90 uses), inconsistent with the rest of the ar locale which keeps "relay" in English.**

- Evidence: Line 251: "**المرحلات هي مكاتب البريد في Nostr.**" and ~90 further occurrences of مرحل/المرحلات throughout faq.mdx. The locale's dominant convention (8 other guides, ar.json UI "الـ Relays", and LESSONS_AR_LOCALE.md "Technical terms may stay in English (… Relay …)") keeps "relay" untranslated. A beginner moving from the FAQ to relay-guide.mdx meets a different word for the protocol's core concept.
- Fix: Align faq.mdx with the locale convention: use "relay/الـ relays" on first mention with a one-time gloss, e.g. "**الـ relays (المُرحّلات) هي مكاتب البريد في Nostr.**", then "relay" thereafter. If المرحلات is instead chosen as the glossary term, apply it locale-wide, not per-file.
**120. [src/content/guides/ar/faq.mdx:324] "Feed" translated as "تغذية" (nutrition/feeding), clashing with the product's own UI term "الخلاصة".**

- Evidence: Line 324 question: "لماذا تغذيتي فارغة؟ أين الجميع؟" (reads like "why is my nutrition empty?"), plus 7 more uses (332, 438, 627, 839 etc.). ar.json uses "الخلاصة" throughout ("مصلح الخلاصة الفارغة" = Empty Feed Fixer, "خلاصة فارغة / لا توجد منشورات"), so the FAQ answer about an empty feed uses a different word than the tool it points to.
- Fix: Replace تغذية/تغذيتي with الخلاصة/خلاصتي in all 8 occurrences in faq.mdx (e.g. "لماذا خلاصتي فارغة؟ أين الجميع؟").
**121. [src/content/guides/ar/faq.mdx:769] Content moderation rendered as "الاعتدال" (temperance/moderateness) instead of the standard "الإشراف على المحتوى".**

- Evidence: Line 769 question: "لماذا الاعتدال على المحتوى مختلف على Nostr؟", and lines 772-774: "ليس لدى Nostr اعتدال مركزي … إنه اعتدال جانب العميل … كيفية عمل الاعتدال". "اعتدال" means being moderate/temperate; "الاعتدال على المحتوى" is also ungrammatical. The established Arabic term for content moderation is "الإشراف على المحتوى".
- Fix: Question → "لماذا يختلف الإشراف على المحتوى في Nostr؟"; body → "ليس لدى Nostr إشراف مركزي على المحتوى", "إشراف من جانب العميل بدلًا من جانب المنصة", heading → "كيف يعمل الإشراف على المحتوى:".
**122. [src/content/guides/ar/finding-community.mdx:505] "Momentum builds slowly" is inverted into "momentum slows down at the start"**

- Evidence: Line 505: "- الزخم يبطئ في البداية" for EN "Momentum builds slowly" (en line 508). يبطئ means "slows down", the opposite of the intended "accumulates gradually".
- Fix: Replace with "- الزخم يتراكم ببطء".
**123. [src/content/guides/ar/finding-community.mdx:464] "Curating content" mistranslated as "توظيف المحتوى" (employing/hiring content)**

- Evidence: Line 464: "- توظيف المحتوى" for EN "Curating content" (en line 467). توظيف means employment/recruitment; the sentence tells readers to "hire content".
- Fix: Replace with "- انتقاء المحتوى الجيد ومشاركته".
**124. [src/content/guides/ar/keys-and-security.mdx:260] "Recently Deleted folder" garbled: the adverb was detached from the folder name, so the horror story no longer explains how the key leaked**

- Evidence: Line 260: "نسيت مجلد المحذوفات مؤخراً" — reads "I recently forgot the deleted-items folder" instead of "I forgot about the ‘Recently Deleted’ folder" (EN line 263: "Forgot about Recently Deleted folder").
- Fix: Replace with "نسيت مجلد «المحذوفة مؤخرًا»" so مؤخرًا is inside the quoted iOS folder name.
**125. [src/content/guides/ar/keys-and-security.mdx:21] Opening sentence is ungrammatical: "Nostr يضع **أنت** مسؤولاً" uses a subject pronoun where Arabic requires an object suffix**

- Evidence: Line 21: "على عكس تويتر أو إنستغرام، Nostr يضع **أنت** مسؤولاً عن هويتك." — a literal calque of "puts **you** in charge"; "يضع أنت" is broken Arabic and it is the first sentence of the security guide.
- Fix: Replace with "على عكس تويتر أو إنستغرام، يجعلك Nostr **أنت** المسؤول عن هويتك." (object suffix ـك plus emphatic أنت keeps the EN bold emphasis).
**126. [src/content/guides/ar/keys-and-security.mdx:256] "توفي الهاتف" says the phone "passed away" — a verb reserved for people, comic in a cautionary quote**

- Evidence: Line 256: "لكني نسيت مزامنته. توفي الهاتف. 2,000 متابع ذهبوا." for EN "Phone died. 2,000 followers gone." (en line 259). توفي is used for human death; a native reader stumbles hard here.
- Fix: Replace "توفي الهاتف. 2,000 متابع ذهبوا." with "تعطّل الهاتف نهائيًا، وضاع 2,000 متابع."
**127. [src/content/guides/ar/multi-client.mdx:176] Broken mixed-script word "الزaps" (Arabic article fused onto Latin "aps") renders as jumbled text.**

- Evidence: Line 176: "**التحقق من التفاعل على الموبايل** (Damus) - الردود، الزaps". The Arabic definite article + ز is fused directly onto Latin letters; BiDi reordering makes the word display broken. EN (line 177): "Replies, zaps".
- Fix: Change to: "الردود والـ zaps" (keeping "zaps" as a standalone Latin token, per the locale convention used in zaps-and-lightning.mdx).
**128. [src/content/guides/ar/multi-client.mdx:104] Relay translated as "الترحيلات" (relocations/deportations), colliding with الترحيل = migration used in the same guide, and diverging from the locale convention of keeping "relay" in English.**

- Evidence: Lines 104-105: "التطبيق أ يرسل إلى الترحيلات / التطبيق ب يتصل بنفس الترحيلات", plus "سرعة الترحيل" (112), "تحكمات ترحيل متقدمة" (223), "اتصالات ترحيل مختلفة" (389), "حالة الترحيل" (392). Meanwhile line 162 "منع المنشورات العرضية" correctly uses the same word for "prevent accidental posts", so one word carries two contradictory EN meanings in the same guide.

**157. [src/content/guides/en/faq.mdx:125] The NIP-05 answer claims the identifier 'proves you control that domain', which is false for the purchased-provider path the same answer recommends.**

- Evidence: "- Can be verified (proves you control that domain)" (line 125). Buying you@nostrplebs.com proves only that the provider mapped the name to your key; you control nothing about the domain. Per the NIP-05 spec, only people who actually own a domain gain "an attestation of their relationship with it".
- Fix: Change the bullet to something like "Can be verified (proves the domain's owner vouches for the mapping — strongest when it's your own domain)".
**158. [src/content/guides/en/faq.mdx:304] The reach answer still implies network-level propagation ('spreads through the network naturally') and gives pre-outbox advice, contradicting the site's own protocol-comparison and outbox-model guides.**

- Evidence: "- Popular content spreads through the network naturally" (line 304), "2. Encourage followers to use the same relays" (line 310), "3. Use NIP-65 (relay recommendations) [ADVANCED]" (line 311). protocol-comparison.mdx line 94: "Relays don't talk to each other; clients aggregate from multiple sources". NIP-65 is 'Relay List Metadata' (the outbox model): followers' clients fetch from YOUR write relays, so herding followers onto your relays is obsolete advice.
- Fix: Rewrite the three bullets: (1) your client publishes each post to all your write relays at once, which is what spreads it; (2) publish a NIP-65 relay list so other clients know where to find your posts (link /guides/outbox-model); drop 'encourage followers to use the same relays'. Rename NIP-65 to 'your public relay list (the outbox model)'.
**159. [src/content/guides/en/faq.mdx:528] nostr.guru, the only named 'Nostr explorer', is a dead domain.**

- Evidence: "- Nostr explorers (nostr.guru)" (line 528, 'Why can't I see my old posts?'). DNS lookup on 2026-09-01: nostr.guru does not resolve.
- Fix: Replace with a live explorer, e.g. njump.me or nostr.band (verify at edit time).
**160. [src/content/guides/en/faq.mdx:648] The backup-your-history answer hands a creator audience developer tooling and mislabels it: nostr-fetch is a JS/TS library, not a CLI tool.**

- Evidence: "Use tools like nostr-fetch or nak to export your events" (line 648) and "2. **Event export** - Use nostr-fetch CLI tool" (line 654), plus "Personal relay - strfry, nostream, khatru" (line 661). nostr-fetch's repo describes it as "A utility library that allows JS/TS apps to effortlessly fetch past events" — there is no nostr-fetch CLI. The audience definition says content requiring dev knowledge is a miss.
- Fix: Lead with what a creator can actually do: back up the nsec, use a client's export/backup feature where offered, and note that some paid relays archive everything you post. Compress the CLI material into one [ADVANCED] line naming a real CLI (nak) and drop the nostr-fetch reference.
**161. [src/content/guides/en/faq.mdx:696] strfry is described as Rust twice; it is written in C++ (C++20).**

- Evidence: "Popular options: strfry (Rust, high performance)" (line 696) and "- **strfry** - Rust, high performance, efficient" (line 700). strfry's repository requires a C++20 compiler; the Rust relay in the same list is nostr-rs-relay.
- Fix: Change both mentions to "strfry (C++, high performance)" — or drop the language labels entirely, since the creator audience gains nothing from them.
**162. [src/content/guides/en/faq.mdx:79] Three 'Learn more' links use anchors that don't exist in the built pages, so they silently land at the top of the target guide.**

- Evidence: Line 79: "(/guides/keys-and-security#the-3-2-1-backup-rule)" — built id is `the-3-2-1-backup-rule-2-minutes`. Line 103: "#understanding-keys" — built id is `understanding-keys-2-minutes`. Line 177: "(/guides/quickstart#choose-your-first-client)" — built ids are `step-2-choose-your-client` / `step-2` (all verified against dist/guides/*/index.html; `check:links` evidently doesn't validate fragments).
- Fix: Update the three fragments to the built ids (#the-3-2-1-backup-rule-2-minutes, #understanding-keys-2-minutes, #step-2-choose-your-client) — or, more robustly, add stable custom ids to those headings so the '(2 minutes)' suffixes can change without breaking inbound anchors. Consider extending check:links to verify fragments.
**163. [src/content/guides/en/faq.mdx:304] FAQ tells beginners 'popular content spreads through the network naturally', contradicting the no-relay-federation teaching in relay-guide and protocol-comparison.**

The 'good news' framing undoes the correct 'Not automatically' answer two lines above it.
- Fix: Replace the bullet with a true mechanism: "Clients usually publish every post to several relays at once, and people who reply or repost you publish those events to their relays too — but relays themselves never copy posts to each other." Keep the NIP-65 bullet that follows.
**164. [src/content/guides/en/faq.mdx:380] FAQ lists paid relay nostr.wine under 'Start with these reliable, free relays'.**

**165. [src/content/guides/en/finding-community.mdx:150] Wisp is listed as a Web client for relay browsing, but its web app (wisp.sh) is gone; Wisp today is an Android/iOS mobile client at wisp.mobile.**

Verified 2026-09-01: https://wisp.sh times out on port 443 (DNS resolves to a Namecheap parking IP 192.64.119.180); Barry Deen's Wisp now lives at wisp.mobile (HTTP 200) as an Android app with an iOS TestFlight beta, positioned as a fast general social client, not a relay-browsing interface.
- Fix: Either drop Wisp from this list or relabel it "(Android/iOS)" with an accurate description. Related out-of-scope fix worth doing in the same pass: src/pages/relay-feed-browser.astro line 130 links the dead https://wisp.sh.
**166. [src/content/guides/en/finding-community.mdx:151] Primal is listed under "Best for relay browsing" but it reads from its own caching server, not user-chosen relays, and has no browse-a-specific-relay feed.**

Its own description here ("Fast and simple") does not even claim relay browsing.

**167. [src/content/guides/en/finding-community.mdx:276] The Community Directories section misdescribes Nostr.info and gives creators no usable tool: the one named site is not a user directory, the "explorer" step names no explorer, and the fallbacks are dev-facing GitHub lists and Twitter threads.**

**169. [src/content/guides/en/finding-community.mdx:399] Fountain is named as the Twitter-Spaces-style live audio tool, but Fountain is a podcast app; the actual Nostr live-audio-rooms app (Nostr Nests) goes unmentioned.**

Fountain is a value-for-value podcasting app; its recent Nostr feature is watching livestreams (v1.1.16), not hosting drop-in audio rooms. Nostr Nests (nostrnests.com, HTTP 200 on 2026-09-01) is "an audio space for chatting, jamming, micro-conferences... powered by Nostr".

While editing, also rename the two stale "Twitter Spaces" labels (lines 366 and 396) to something like "Live audio rooms (like X/Twitter Spaces)".
**170. [src/content/guides/en/finding-community.mdx:17] The guide is ~95% bullet fragments with almost no flowing prose; only the relay-feeds intro reads as written text.**

Of ~550 lines, only lines 100-111 ("Hashtags connect you to topics across all of Nostr. But what if...") are connected prose. Every other section is stacked fragment lists.

Quick Reference may legitimately stay a list.
**171. [src/content/guides/en/finding-community.mdx:412] DM etiquette section states DM metadata is visible as a blanket fact, contradicting the NIP-17 guide it never links to.**

- Evidence: "**Nostr DMs are encrypted but not perfect:** - End-to-end encrypted (E2EE) - But metadata visible (who talks to whom) - Some clients handle encryption differently" (DM Etiquette, lines ~410-415), plus "Remember it's still public metadata" (line 426). This describes deprecated NIP-04; nip17-private-messages.mdx teaches that NIP-17 hides the sender (recipient and timing still visible) and the FAQ calls NIP-04 deprecated. finding-community is read in the beginner path long before the NIP-17 guide and never hands off to it.
- Fix: Reword to: "Older NIP-04 DMs leak who talks to whom; modern clients use NIP-17, which hides the sender but still shows who received a message and when", and add a 'learn more' link to /guides/nip17-private-messages. Keep the 'don't send sensitive info' advice unchanged.
**172. [src/content/guides/en/keys-and-security.mdx:255] Fabricated testimonials and case studies are presented to readers as real events.**

- Evidence: "These are real. Learn from them:" (line 255) introducing quotes attributed to invented handles "@nostruser123" and "@learnedthehardway"; and "here are real examples of what happened when people didn't follow it" (line 304) introducing three unverifiable case studies including a specific "$50,000" figure.
- Fix: Reframe both sections honestly: "These scenarios are composites of mistakes people commonly make on Nostr" (or similar), drop the fake @handles and the invented dollar figure, and remove both "real" claims. The safety lessons stand on their own without fabricated provenance.
**173. [src/content/guides/en/keys-and-security.mdx:103] The key generator's status flips between 'simulator to practice with', 'save your nsec NOW', and 'real test keys', so a beginner cannot tell if the generated key is a toy or their real identity.**

- Evidence: Line 103: "Think of this tool as a 'simulator' to practice with before using a real client." Lines 113-116: "Save Both Keys Immediately... Copy your nsec (red) to a SECURE place. Do this NOW before doing anything else." Line 141: "Use the Key Generator above for real test keys." ("real test keys" is self-contradictory). Also lines 118-121: "Verify You Saved Them - Close the generator - Reopen it - Can you still access your keys?" reads as if reopening restores the keys.
- Fix: The component (src/components/interactive/KeyGenerator.tsx) generates real secp256k1 keys via nostr-tools, so say so plainly: "This generator creates a real, valid key pair in your browser. You can keep it as your actual Nostr identity, or throw it away and generate a fresh one in your client later. If you keep it, back up the nsec now; if you treat it as practice, never reuse it for your real account." Replace "real test keys" with one consistent term, and reword the verification step to "close the generator, then confirm you can read both keys from the place you saved them".
**174. [src/content/guides/en/keys-and-security.mdx:36] KeyVisualizer renders eye-toggle and copy buttons that do nothing when clicked.**

- Evidence: Line 36: "<KeyVisualizer />" (no client directive, no props). The rendered component src/components/ui/KeyVisualizer.tsx wires its show/hide button to onClick={onTogglePrivate} and its copy buttons to onClick={() => onCopy?.(type)}; both props are undefined here and the component is never hydrated, so all three buttons are inert dead controls in the 'What Is a Key Pair?' section.
- Fix: Either wrap it in a small stateful component that handles toggle/copy internally (with navigator.clipboard) and mount it with client:idle, or add a static variant that renders the two key rows without any buttons. Do not ship visible controls that never respond.
**175. [src/content/guides/en/multi-client.mdx:127] Mute-list advice is stale 2023-era: 'NIP-51 standardizing this. May need manual sync for now'**

- Evidence: Workarounds for Non-Syncing Data (lines 124-128): '**Mutes/Blocks:** - Some clients export/import lists - NIP-51 standardizing this - May need manual sync for now', echoed by '✗ Mute/block lists (sometimes)' at line 94. NIP-51 has defined the kind 10000 mute list for years and Damus, Amethyst, Primal, Snort and Coracle all read/write it, so mute lists sync automatically between major clients in 2026; export/import is not the mechanism.
- Fix: Rewrite the Mutes/Blocks workaround to: mute lists are a Nostr standard (NIP-51) and sync automatically between all major clients; only app-local blocks or per-app 'hide' settings stay local. Adjust line 94 from '(sometimes)' to something like 'app-local blocks only'.
**176. [src/content/guides/en/multi-client.mdx:108] Syncing section promises 'Everything stays in sync' without stating the relay-list condition or the follow-list overwrite risk**

- Evidence: The Syncing Process (lines 104-108): '3. Client B connects to same relays 4. Client B sees your post 5. Everything stays in sync', with '✓ Follows list' listed as unconditionally automatic (line 83). Nothing says clients must share or discover your relay list (that is exactly what NIP-65 relay lists do, covered in the site's own outbox-model guide), and the guide's own troubleshooting section (lines 388-399) admits 'Different relay connections' and 'Follows different'. The classic multi-client failure, a long-offline client overwriting your kind-3 follow list with a stale copy, is never warned about.
- Fix: Add two sentences to 'The Syncing Process': sync works because clients find your posts on the relays in your published relay list (NIP-65), so keep that list consistent, with a link to /guides/outbox-model. In Troubleshooting, add a warning: when reopening a client you have not used in months, let it fully refresh before following or unfollowing anyone, or it can overwrite your follow list with an old copy. Soften step 5 to 'your content and follows stay in sync'.
**177. [src/content/guides/en/multi-client.mdx:33] 'Public client / Private client' use case implies a second client creates a private identity, without mentioning it requires a second keypair**

- Evidence: Common Use Cases (lines 31-34): '**Public + Private:** - **Public client:** Professional identity, public posts - **Private client:** Personal identity, different circles'. The guide's whole premise is one identity across clients, so a beginner reading this will assume installing a second client gives them a separate, more private presence. It does not: same npub, same public posts, and the word 'Private' suggests privacy that no client provides.
- Fix: Reword to make the key the unit of identity: 'Two identities: some people keep a second keypair for a personal account and dedicate a different client to each one - the separation comes from having two sets of keys, not from the app.' Avoid the label 'Private client'.
**178. [src/content/guides/en/multi-client.mdx:370] Advanced Workflows and Automation sections are contentless category lists, including a Developer persona in a creators' guide**

- Evidence: Lines 341-380: 'Analytics: Third-party tools' appears twice with no tool named, 'Tools: - Nostr bots - Cross-posting services - Scheduling tools - Analytics platforms' names zero actual products, and lines 353-358 devote a whole persona to '**Developer:** - Testing: Multiple clients simultaneously - Debugging: Developer tools' although the site's stated audience is creators, not developers. A creator finishing this section has learned nothing actionable.
- Fix: Cut the Developer persona. Either name real tools for the remaining bullets (linking to /guides/nostr-tools, which the guide already references in Keep reading) or delete the Automation subsection entirely and fold one sentence about scheduling/cross-posting into the Content Creator workflow.
**179. [src/content/guides/en/multi-client.mdx:18] The guide is almost entirely bullet fragments; only two sentences of flowing prose outside callouts**

- Evidence: From '## Why Multiple Clients?' (line 18) to Quick Reference (line 419), every section is fragment bullets ('Use best tool for each task', 'Mobile for consuming', 'Wait and refresh'); the only running prose is the single paragraph at line 22 and the two Callout boxes. This is exactly the ~90% bullet-fragment pattern the July 2026 audit flagged.
- Fix: Rewrite into prose, keeping at most one list per section: 'Why Multiple Clients?' (turn the use-case lists into two short paragraphs with a creator example), 'Syncing Strategy' (explain the relay mechanism in a paragraph, keep the syncs/doesn't-sync pair as one compact list), 'Desktop + Mobile' (narrate the Content Creator workflow as a day-in-the-life paragraph), 'Client-Specific Features' (one sentence per client instead of four fragments), 'Backup & Migration' and 'Troubleshooting' (prose with a single checklist each).
**180. [src/content/guides/en/nip05-identity.mdx:434] Verification method sends readers to nostr.guru, a dead domain that no longer resolves in DNS.**

- Evidence: "**Method 2: Nostr Explorer** 1. Visit a Nostr explorer like nostr.guru 2. Search for your NIP-05" (Advanced Tips → Verifying Your Setup, ~line 434). Verified 2026-09-01: getaddrinfo ENOTFOUND nostr.guru.
- Fix: Replace nostr.guru with njump.me and make the step concrete: "Open njump.me/yourname@yourdomain.com — if it shows your profile, your NIP-05 resolves." (Verified: njump.me/dergigi@dergigi.com resolves to the correct profile as of 2026-09-01.)
**181. [src/content/guides/en/nip05-identity.mdx:253] Self-hosting setup and troubleshooting omit two spec-mandated server requirements: the CORS header and the no-redirects rule.**

- Evidence: Step 2 (~lines 230-255) and the "Verification Failed" causes (~lines 335-349) list JSON syntax, file location, caching, and HTTPS, but never mention CORS or redirects. NIP-05 spec: servers should send "Access-Control-Allow-Origin: *" for browser-based clients, and the endpoint must not return redirects (fetchers ignore them). A self-hoster whose file opens fine in a browser will still fail verification in web clients (Primal web, Coracle, Iris) without the CORS header, and an apex→www redirect silently breaks verification — the two failure modes most likely to hit exactly the person this section targets.
- Fix: In Step 2, after the "No DNS records are involved" paragraph, add: the server must send the header `Access-Control-Allow-Origin: *` on this file (one line in most hosts' config), and the URL must respond directly — a redirect (e.g. yourdomain.com → www.yourdomain.com) breaks verification. Add both as causes 5 and 6 under the "Verification Failed" troubleshooting entry.
**182. [src/content/guides/en/nip05-identity.mdx:115] Free Options section carries editing artifacts: the first provider is introduced as "Another great free option", and NostrCheck is listed twice.**

- Evidence: "### NostrCheck\n\nAnother great free option." (~lines 115-117) and under Pros "- Also free" (~line 128) — both reference a preceding provider that no longer exists in the text. Then "Other Free Providers" repeats it: "- **NostrCheck.me** - Additional tools included" (~line 136). Same list also uses unexplained jargon: "**stacker.news**: For SN users" (line 135).
- Fix: Reword the NostrCheck intro to stand alone ("A free, community-run option.") and change "Also free" to "Free". Delete the duplicate NostrCheck.me bullet from "Other Free Providers". Expand "For SN users" to "if you already use Stacker News".
**183. [src/content/guides/en/nip05-identity.mdx:271] The own-domain path assumes dev knowledge the creator audience does not have: no hosting step exists, and troubleshooting leans on command-line tools.**

- Evidence: Step 2 jumps straight to "Host a file at https://yourdomain.com/.well-known/nostr.json" and "2. **Upload to your server:**" (~line 271) without ever saying the reader needs web hosting; troubleshooting then assumes "Check with `dig` or `nslookup` command" (~line 327), "Check `.htaccess` or server config" (~line 362), "curl" (~line 441), "Check server logs ... file permissions" (~lines 376-377). A writer or musician who owns a domain through Squarespace/Wix/Substack often cannot upload to /.well-known/ at all — the guide never warns them.
- Fix: Between Step 1 and Step 2, add a short paragraph: you need somewhere that serves files for your domain (your existing website); note explicitly that many site builders (Squarespace, Wix, and similar) cannot serve /.well-known/ files, and that in that case a free or paid provider is the practical choice. In troubleshooting, lead with "paste your address into the checker on this page" and demote dig/nslookup/curl to an optional aside for technical readers.
**184. [src/content/guides/en/nip05-identity.mdx:39] The guide is roughly 95% bullet fragments and tables with almost no flowing prose; four sections most need rewriting into prose.**

- Evidence: Outside two Callouts and the hex-key paragraphs (~lines 242-255), nearly every section is fragment bullets, e.g. "- ✅ **Easy to share**: 'Find me at bob@nostrplebs.com'" (line 40) and "- Building a personal brand / - Creating content / - Running a business" (~lines 94-97). Matches the July 2026 audit pattern (~90% bullets).
- Fix: Rewrite into connected prose: (1) "What is NIP-05?" + "What You Get" (lines 17-49) as one explanatory passage; (2) "Why Get a NIP-05?" — especially "Trust & Authenticity" and "Not Centralization!" (lines 74-107), which argue claims that need sentences, not fragments; (3) the "Free Options" intro and provider blurbs (lines 111-146); (4) "Privacy Considerations" (lines 450-455), where the deanonymization warning deserves a real paragraph. Leave the troubleshooting lists and pricing tables as reference material.
**185. [src/content/guides/en/nip05-identity.mdx:472] Resources links to github.com/nostr-tools/nip05, a repository that does not exist (404).**

- Evidence: "- [NIP05 Generator](https://github.com/nostr-tools/nip05) - DIY tools" (~line 472). Verified 2026-09-01: both github.com/nostr-tools/nip05 and api.github.com/repos/nostr-tools/nip05 return 404 (public repos return 200 unauthenticated, so the repo does not exist).
- Fix: Delete the bullet. It is a dev-oriented resource in a creators' guide anyway; the guide's own on-page checker and the linked /tools/nip05-checker cover the DIY-testing need.
**186. [src/content/guides/en/nip05-identity.mdx] Linked GitHub path github.com/nostr-tools/nip05 does not exist (404)**

- Evidence: WebFetch of https://github.com/nostr-tools/nip05 returned HTTP 404 Not Found. The actual NIP-05 implementation lives in the nbd-wtf/nostr-tools repo, not an org/repo named nostr-tools/nip05.
- Fix: Fix the link to point to https://github.com/nbd-wtf/nostr-tools (and reference the nip05 module within it), since no repo exists at nostr-tools/nip05.
**187. [src/content/guides/en/nip17-private-messages.mdx:60] Randomized timestamps are labeled 'replay protection', which is not what they are for**

- Evidence: Comparison table, line 60: "| **Replay Protection**  | None | Randomised timestamps |"; and line 106 (seal layer): "Has its own timestamp for replay protection". Per NIP-17, created_at is randomized "up to two days in the past... to make sure grouping by `created_at` doesn't reveal any metadata" — it defeats timing correlation, not replays.
- Fix: Rename the table row to "Timing metadata" with values "Real timestamps visible" / "Timestamps randomised up to 2 days back", and change line 106 to say the seal's timestamp is randomised so messages can't be grouped by time.
**188. [src/content/guides/en/nip17-private-messages.mdx:327] The 'unreadable or gibberish' failure mode is misdescribed: a non-NIP-17 recipient sees nothing at all, as the guide's own 10050 section explains**

- Evidence: Lines 327-330: "### \"Messages show as unreadable or gibberish\" / **Cause**: Recipient doesn't support NIP-17 / **Solution**: ... temporarily switch back to NIP-04"; same claim at line 270: "Sending NIP-17 to a NIP-04-only client will result in unreadable messages." A NIP-04-only client never fetches kind 1059 and has no kind 10050 list, so per spec the sender's client "shouldn't try" — the real symptom is silent non-delivery, exactly what the guide's own troubleshooting item at lines 307-325 describes. The 'switch back to NIP-04' advice also reintroduces the metadata leak the guide exists to remove, without a warning.
- Fix: Merge this item into the silent-non-delivery item: "if the recipient's client has no NIP-17 support, your message is never delivered, nothing shows as gibberish". If the NIP-04 fallback advice stays, add one sentence that falling back re-exposes who-talks-to-whom metadata and should be a last resort for non-sensitive chats. Apply the same correction to line 270.
**189. [src/content/guides/en/nip17-private-messages.mdx:365] Literal placeholder identifiers 'nostr:npub1...' and 'nostr:note1...' render to readers as unfinished content**

- Evidence: Lines 365-366: "- nostr:npub1... # Nostr protocol development / - nostr:npub1... # Security best practices" under "Stay updated by following:" — a reader cannot follow an elided npub. Line 208, inside the copy-paste migration template code block: "nostr:note1... # Migration message template" — anyone copying the template copies the placeholder line too.
- Fix: Delete the two-bullet "Stay updated by following" block (or substitute real, checked npubs), and remove the "nostr:note1... # Migration message template" line from the code block so the template starts at "Hey! I'm upgrading...".
**190. [src/content/guides/en/nip17-private-messages.mdx:219] Verification steps assume the reader can inspect raw events, which the creator audience cannot do and the guide never teaches**

- Evidence: Step 5, lines 219-221: "2. Check the event kind (should be `1059` for gift wraps) / 3. Look for the `kind: 1059` in message details". No mainstream creator-facing client exposes 'the event kind' of a DM, and the guide gives no tool or menu for finding 'message details'. Same problem at line 347: "1. Check relay's supported NIPs" with no explanation of how.
- Fix: Replace the raw-event checks with a client-level verification: send a test DM between two NIP-17 clients (or to yourself from a second client) and confirm it arrives both ways; optionally add one concrete inspectable path (e.g. a client that shows an encryption badge on the message). For line 347, either name a concrete way to check a relay (its /.well-known NIP-11 page via a relay-info site) or drop that sub-step.
**191. [src/content/guides/en/nostr-tools.mdx:188] Zap Stream is mischaracterized as a zap-notification tool; zap.stream is a live-streaming platform.**

- Evidence: "**Zap Stream** - **What it does:** Real-time zap notifications - **Best for:** Live events, streams" (Zap Tools, lines 188-190). zap.stream's site describes itself as "Nostr Live Streaming" (verified 2026-09-01).

- Evidence: Table row: "| **Identity** | NostrPlebs | NIP-05 identifier | Free/Paid |" (line 317) vs body lines 86-87: "**Cost:** Paid only - 12,500-65,000 sats/year ... **Note:** No free tier available". Verified 2026-09-01: nostrplebs.com lists 65k/45k/22.5k/12.5k sats by name length, no free option.

**193. [src/content/guides/en/nostr-tools.mdx:320] Table lists Alby Hub as "Free"; the hosted Alby Cloud costs about $9.90-12.90/month, only self-hosting is free.**

- Evidence: "| **Wallet** | Alby Hub | Web users | Free |" (line 320). getalby.com/pricing: Alby Cloud is a paid subscription (~$9.9/month, 21,000 sats/month, first 3 months discounted); running Alby Hub yourself is free but requires a server or Raspberry Pi, which is not a creator-friendly path.
- Fix: Change Cost to "Paid cloud / free self-hosted", and consider whether Alby Hub belongs in a beginners' quick table at all next to zero-setup options like Primal Wallet or Coinos.
**194. [src/content/guides/en/nostr-tools.mdx:65] NostrImg entry is stale: nostrimg.com now just 307-redirects to nostr.build.**

- Evidence: "**NostrImg** - **URL:** [nostrimg.com](https://nostrimg.com) - **What it does:** Simple image hosting service - **Features:** Fast uploads, permanent storage" (lines 65-68). Verified 2026-09-01: https://nostrimg.com returns 307 Temporary Redirect to https://nostr.build/, so it is no longer an independent service and the "permanent storage" description is unfounded.

**195. [src/content/guides/en/nostr-tools.mdx:45] YubiKey recommended for nsec storage, but no mainstream Nostr flow can store or sign with a key on a YubiKey.**

- Evidence: "**Key Storage Best Practices** - Password managers (Bitwarden, 1Password) - Hardware security keys (YubiKey)" (Backup & Recovery, lines 43-47). YubiKeys hold FIDO/OTP credentials; no beginner Nostr client signs events with one. A creator following this buys hardware that cannot hold their nsec in any usable way.
- Fix: Remove the YubiKey bullet. Keep password manager, paper backup, and encrypted USB; optionally add "a signer app like Amber (Android) so your nsec never touches other apps" as the practical hardware-adjacent option.
**196. [src/content/guides/en/nostr-tools.mdx:209] Large developer-only sections in a guide whose audience is creators.**
