Roughly 45 lines serve developers; the project rule says content requiring dev knowledge is a miss for this audience.
- Fix: Cut the Developer Tools and Relay Software sections, replacing both with one line: "Building something on Nostr? The community keeps a developer list at github.com/aljazceru/awesome-nostr." Reframe NAK's entry (line 35 "Best for: Developers and power users") or drop it too.
**197. [src/content/guides/en/nostr-tools.mdx:37] Nine directory entries have no URL or only "search for it yourself" guidance, so readers cannot follow them.**

- Evidence: "**Nostr Key Generator** - **What it does:** Generate new key pairs offline" (no URL, lines 37-39); "**Relay Tools** - Various community-maintained relay lists" (137-139); "**ZapSplits**" (no URL, 184-186); "**Nostr Debug** - Various tools ... Check GitHub for latest tools" (238-240); "**Other Signer Apps** - ... Search your app store for 'Nostr signer'" (264-267); "**Awesome Nostr** - GitHub repository" with no link (292-294); "**Nostr Directory** - ... Check nostr.social or similar aggregators" (296-298; verified: nostr.social is a did:nostr DID-document indexer, not a people/tools directory a creator can use); "**Telegram/Discord Groups** - ... Search for 'Nostr'" (306-308). In a guide whose whole value is being a directory, entries without destinations are filler.
- Fix: For each entry: add a real, verified URL (e.g. link Awesome Nostr to github.com/aljazceru/awesome-nostr, explain zap splits as a client feature in Amethyst/zap.stream rather than a product) or delete the entry. Remove the nostr.social pointer entirely.
**198. [src/content/guides/en/nostr-tools.mdx] Linked relay framework khatru is archived and its maintainer recommends an alternative**

- Evidence: WebFetch of https://github.com/fiatjaf/khatru: 'The project was archived on January 24, 2026, and is now read-only... the maintainer notes... recommends that developers consider trying an alternative version with some new features instead.'
- Fix: Note the archived status or swap the link for the maintainer-recommended successor project so readers aren't pointed at an unmaintained relay framework.
**199. [src/content/guides/en/outbox-model.mdx:104] Scenario 2 shows the same two relays from Scenario 1 with flipped markers, undermining the read/write lesson**

- Evidence: Scenario 1 (lines 89-91) defines "Alice's Relay List (kind:10002): - wss://relay.a.com [write] - wss://relay.b.com [write]". Scenario 2 (lines 104-106) then shows "Charlie's client checks Alice's relay list: - wss://relay.a.com [read] - wss://relay.b.com [read]" - identical URLs, opposite markers, right after the guide taught that read and write mean different things (lines 77-80).
- Fix: Give Alice one consistent list used by both scenarios, e.g. Scenario 1: relay.a.com [write], relay.b.com [write], relay.inbox.com [read]; Scenario 2: Charlie's client finds the [read] entry and sends the mention to wss://relay.inbox.com. This also demonstrates why the read marker exists.
**200. [src/content/guides/en/outbox-model.mdx:153] Four guides give a beginner four conflicting relay-count targets; outbox-model even tells readers to undo what relay-guide told them to do.**

- Evidence: outbox-model.mdx: "**Recommended:** 2-4 relays total" (line 153) and "**If you have 5+ relays:** Remove the ones with poor uptime" (line 234). relays-demystified.mdx: "**Connect to 4-8 relays** for good coverage" (line 207). relay-guide.mdx: "Beginner 3-5, Active 5-10, Power 10-15" and "**Recommended Count:** 5-10 relays" (line 571). faq.mdx: "**Sweet spot:** 5-10 quality relays" (line 275). NIP-65 itself says "keep kind:10002 lists small (2-4 relays of each category)" — per category, not total. Reading the site in path order, the user is told 4-8, then to trim to 2-4, then to grow to 5-10.
- Fix: Pick one message and apply it everywhere; the least-churn option: fix outbox-model to quote NIP-65 correctly ("2-4 write relays and 2-4 read relays — roughly 4-8 total") and change the 'If you have 5+ relays' trigger to 10+, which makes it consistent with relays-demystified's 4-8 and compatible with relay-guide/FAQ's 5-10. No edits then needed in the other three files.
**201. [src/content/guides/en/privacy-security.mdx:181] Primal is listed under 'Popular Signer Apps' although the bullets describe an ordinary client holding your nsec, negating the section's own security promise**

- Evidence: "**Primal (iOS)** - Built-in secure storage - No need for separate signer app - Keys stay on device" (lines 181-186) sits under '### Popular Signer Apps', in a section whose benefits are "Nsec never leaves signer, Clients can't steal keys, Compromised client = safe keys" (lines 156-158). If the client stores the key itself, none of those isolation properties hold for that client. The entry also misses what actually qualifies Primal here: v3.0 (March 2026) added 'Remote Login' (NIP-46), letting Primal sign into other web clients without exposing the key.
- Fix: Reframe the Primal entry honestly: on iOS there is no Amber equivalent, so Primal keeps keys in the device keychain (weaker isolation than a dedicated signer), and since v3.0 it can act as a NIP-46 remote signer ('Remote Login') for web clients, which is the feature that belongs in this section. Drop 'No need for separate signer app' as a benefit.
**202. [src/content/guides/en/privacy-security.mdx:452] The compromise-recovery template tells readers to post a message 'signed by both keys', which no client can produce and which proves nothing after a compromise**

- Evidence: "This message is signed by both keys to prove transition.\n\n[Signed message from new key]" (code block, lines 445-457). A Nostr event carries exactly one signature; no mainstream client lets a beginner embed a second key's signature in a note. Worse, if the old key is compromised, the attacker holds it too, so an old-key signature is not proof of anything.
- Fix: Replace the template with actionable steps: post the alert from BOTH accounts, each mentioning the other npub; update NIP-05 on a domain you control to the new key; confirm the move through out-of-band channels (website, other socials, direct messages to close contacts). Remove the 'signed by both keys' line entirely.
**203. [src/content/guides/en/privacy-security.mdx:18] The guide is essentially 100% bullet fragments; six teaching sections need rewriting into flowing prose**

- Evidence: Outside the four Callouts and the closing line "Security is a journey, not a destination" (line 494), every section is telegraphic bullets, e.g. 'Threat Model': "- **Concern:** Don't want employers/family seeing everything - **Threats:** Public posts, profile association - **Solution:** Separate identities" (lines 26-28). This matches the July 2026 audit's ~90%-bullets pattern.
- Fix: Rewrite into real prose (with bullets kept only where they earn it): 'Threat Model', 'Identity Separation', 'Signer Apps', 'Key Rotation', 'Metadata Leaks', 'Recovery Planning'. 'OPSEC Checklist' and 'Quick Reference' are legitimately list-shaped and can stay. Metadata Leaks especially deserves narrative treatment for creators (writing style and timing deanonymization is a story, not a taxonomy).
**204. [src/content/guides/en/privacy-security.mdx:34] Security jargon is used on a creator audience without a single definition: OPSEC, air-gapped, HSM, custodial, device fingerprints**

- Evidence: "**Solution:** Anonymous account, OPSEC" (line 34) is the first of ~8 uses of OPSEC, never expanded; also "air-gapped keys" (lines 40, 398), "Hardware security modules" (line 399), "Moving from custodial to self-custodial" (line 237), "Device fingerprints" (line 310), and "**Nostr Connect** (cross-platform protocol)" (line 190) listed as if it were an app.
- Fix: Add one-line plain-language glosses at first use, e.g. "OPSEC (operational security): the everyday habits that keep your identities from leaking into each other"; "air-gapped: generated on a device that never touches the internet". Change the Nostr Connect bullet to "remote signing (NIP-46, sometimes called Nostr Connect) built into some clients" so it reads as a capability, not a product.
**205. [src/content/guides/en/privacy-security.mdx:181] Primal is presented as a signer app in two guides, contradicting the guides' own definition of what a signer protects against.**

- Evidence: privacy-security.mdx, under "Popular Signer Apps": "**Primal (iOS)** - Built-in secure storage - No need for separate signer app - Keys stay on device" (lines ~181-186), directly after defining signers as "Client app never sees your nsec". nostr-tools.mdx repeats it as a product that does not exist: "**Primal Signer (iOS)** - Built into Primal app" (lines ~252-256). Primal is a client that holds the key itself — the exact arrangement the signer section teaches away from — and no other guide (quickstart, keys-and-security) lists it among signers (Alby, nos2x, Nostore, nsec.app, Amber).
- Fix: In privacy-security.mdx, move Primal out of 'Popular Signer Apps' into a one-line note ("iOS has no Amber equivalent; iOS clients like Damus/Primal store the key in the system keychain instead") or list nsec.app (NIP-46) as the cross-platform option quickstart already recommends. In nostr-tools.mdx, delete the fictional 'Primal Signer (iOS)' entry or retitle it the same way.
**206. [src/content/guides/en/protocol-comparison.mdx:551] Footer links to a fabricated placeholder Nostr profile, npub1beginner, which is not even valid bech32**

- Evidence: Line 551: "Join the discussion on [Nostr](https://primal.net/p/npub1beginner)". "npub1beginner" cannot be a real npub: the characters b and i are outside the bech32 charset (qpzry9x8gf2tvdw0s3jn54khce6mua7l), so the link always lands on a broken profile page. The same placeholder is present in all six translations of this guide.
- Fix: Replace the URL with the site's real account (ptrio42's npub on primal.net) or link internally to /follow-pack/ ("find people to follow on Nostr"). Apply in en plus the six translations. Getting ptrio42's confirmation on which npub to use is enough; do not invent one.
**207. [src/content/guides/en/protocol-comparison.mdx:384] Graysky is recommended as a best Bluesky client but has been in maintenance mode since March 2024**

- Evidence: Line 384: "**Best Clients:** Official Bluesky app, Graysky (mobile), Tokimeki (web)". Verified: Graysky's developer joined Bluesky's team in March 2024 and stated the app would get no "ambitious new features"; it is still on the stores but effectively frozen. TOKIMEKI (tokimeki.blue) is verified live and actively developed.
- Fix: Drop Graysky: "**Best Clients:** Official Bluesky app, TOKIMEKI (web, multi-column)". Optionally add one sentence that most third-party Bluesky clients stalled after Bluesky hired their developers.
**208. [src/content/guides/en/protocol-comparison.mdx:399] "No algorithms, no virality—just chronological feeds" misstates 2026 Nostr, where algorithmic feeds exist but are user-chosen**

- Evidence: Line 399: "**Embrace the differences:** No algorithms, no virality—just chronological feeds". Primal ships trending/explore feeds, Amethyst has discovery feeds, and DVM-powered algorithmic feeds are an established part of the ecosystem.
- Fix: Rewrite to: "**Embrace the differences:** Feeds are chronological by default; algorithmic and trending feeds exist, but you pick them — no platform imposes one on you."
**209. [src/content/guides/en/protocol-comparison.mdx:372] The guide steers the site's own audience (creators) to Bluesky and never mentions zaps or creator monetization, Nostr's main creator draw**

- Evidence: Line 26: "**Best For** | Activists, cypherpunks, Bitcoiners" (Nostr). Lines 372-376: "### Choose Bluesky If... ✅ **You're a content creator or public figure**". The word "zap" never appears; Lightning is mentioned only as a builder concern ("You're building in the Bitcoin ecosystem", line 334) and a roadmap bullet (line 475). For nostrich.love's declared audience (writers, artists, musicians), the comparison omits Nostr's strongest creator argument: direct audience payments with no platform cut, plus long-form and music clients.
- Fix: Add a creator block under "Choose Nostr If...": zaps as direct income from readers/listeners with no platform fee, long-form publishing, music and art clients. Change the "Best For" cell for Nostr to include "independent creators" alongside the current groups, and soften the Bluesky creator bullet to "you want mainstream reach and domain verification" so the two blocks are complementary rather than sending creators away.
**210. [src/content/guides/en/quickstart.mdx:190] The nsec.app link is currently unreachable (root domain fails from two vantage points) and the project behind it has been stale since May 2025.**

- Evidence: "a remote signer such as [nsec.app](https://nsec.app) (NIP-46)" (line 190). Verified 2026-09-01: WebFetch gets ECONNREFUSED on https://nsec.app and local curl times out after 20s on 95.216.33.150:443, while https://use.nsec.app (the actual app) returns 200. The backing repo nostrband/noauth was last pushed 2025-05-26 with 46 open issues (GitHub API).
- Fix: Point the link at https://use.nsec.app so the click actually lands on the working app (same change in all 7 locale quickstarts, grep nsec.app). Separately decide whether a project unmaintained for 15 months should stay the sole NIP-46 recommendation; if not, mention Amber (Android) as the maintained signer alternative for phone users.
**211. [src/content/guides/en/quickstart.mdx:177] "A mobile app is a sandbox — only that app can read what you paste into it" is a false safety promise: the pasted nsec travels through the system clipboard, which is outside the app sandbox.**

- Evidence: "**On a phone**, paste your nsec into the app once. A mobile app is a sandbox — only that app can read what you paste into it." (lines 177-178). Keyboards with cloud clipboard sync (Gboard, SwiftKey) upload clipboard contents to their servers, iOS Universal Clipboard shares it with nearby devices, and any foreground app can read the clipboard while the nsec sits on it.
- Fix: Keep the paste-into-app recommendation (it is standard practice) but drop the absolute claim and add one caveat sentence, e.g.: "A mobile app keeps what you type inside its own sandbox. The clipboard is the weak spot: paste your nsec once, then copy something else over it, and turn off any keyboard cloud-clipboard sync first." Mirror the fix in the other locale quickstarts.
**212. [src/content/guides/en/quickstart.mdx:182] nos2x is linked to its GitHub repository, a dead end for the site's non-developer audience who cannot install an extension from a code repo.**

- Evidence: "[nos2x](https://github.com/fiatjaf/nos2x)" (line 182), in a step instructing creators to "Install a signer extension instead". The target is a source-code page with no one-click install for a non-technical reader.
- Fix: Link the Chrome Web Store listing instead: https://chromewebstore.google.com/detail/nos2x/kpgefcfmnafjgpblomihpgmejjdanjjp (verified live via search results 2026-09-01). Same change in all locale quickstarts (grep github.com/fiatjaf/nos2x).
**213. [src/content/guides/en/relay-guide.mdx:187] "Connect to these 5 relays" is followed by a list of only 4 relays, one of which is a paid, optional relay.**

- Evidence: Line 187: "Connect to these 5 relays for good coverage:" followed by the code block at lines 189-194 containing exactly four entries: relay.damus.io, nos.lol, relay.snort.social, nostr.wine, with the last marked "(Quality paid option - optional)".
- Fix: Either change the text to "these 3 free relays (plus one optional paid relay)" or add a fifth free relay to the block. Keep the count and the list in sync with whatever replacement is chosen for relay.snort.social (see the 503 finding).
**214. [src/content/guides/en/relay-guide.mdx:295] The four client-specific relay walkthroughs contain stale or invented UI paths that will mislead beginners.**

- Evidence: Lines 295-301, Damus: "1. Profile tab → Settings (gear icon)" — Damus has no Profile tab; settings are reached through the sidebar opened from your avatar (Damus support docs describe reaching relays via Settings from the sidebar). Lines 311-317, Iris: "Click profile picture → Settings → 'Relays' tab" — Iris was fully rebuilt (irislib/iris-client replaced the old codebase in October 2024), so these steps describe a client that no longer exists in that form. The Amethyst and Primal step lists are similarly unverifiable release-specific paths.
- Fix: Replace the four step-by-step client blocks with the already-present generic steps (lines 278-291) plus one sentence per client naming where relay settings live in broad terms ("in Damus and Amethyst look under Settings, in Primal under Settings → Network"), and link to the client simulators at sandstr.app for click-through practice (linking there is an approved pattern). If exact steps are kept, they must be re-verified against current app versions each audit.
**215. [src/content/guides/en/relay-guide.mdx:5] estimatedTime says 12 minutes but the guide's own section timers add up to 29 minutes.**

- Evidence: Frontmatter line 5: estimatedTime: "12 minutes". Section headings carry their own timers: "How Relays Work (3 minutes)", "Types of Relays (2 minutes)", "Choosing Relays (5 minutes)", "Adding Relays (3 minutes)", "Paid Relays (3 minutes)", "Your Own Relay [ADVANCED] (10 minutes)", "Troubleshooting (3 minutes)" = 29 minutes, plus two untimed sections (Relay Testing & Exploration with the RelayPlayground, and the quiz).
- Fix: Set estimatedTime to "20-25 minutes" (other guides already use ranges, e.g. "10-15 minutes") or drop the per-section timers so there is one source of truth. Update the commented-out ProgressIndicator line 18, which repeats "12 minutes".
**216. [src/content/guides/en/relay-guide.mdx:397] "Your Own Relay" is a 90-line sysadmin tutorial (apt, git clone, npm, nano, PostgreSQL, nginx, SSL) in a product whose stated audience is non-technical creators.**

- Evidence: Lines 397-484: prerequisites "Ubuntu/Debian server, Node.js 18+, PostgreSQL", a bash block with "sudo apt update / sudo apt install nodejs npm postgresql / git clone ... / nano .env", then "Set up SSL (Let's Encrypt), Configure reverse proxy (nginx)". Project rule: content requiring dev knowledge is a miss even when accurate. Secondary staleness: Node.js 18 has been end-of-life since April 2025, and nostream's repo (checked today, pushed 2026-09-01, not archived) is still alive so the software picks are fine, but the walkthrough is unusable by the target reader.
- Fix: Cut the bash walkthrough and the Prerequisites/Connect domain/Maintenance sub-lists. Keep "Why Run Your Own?" and the three software names as a one-paragraph orientation ("it takes a server, a domain and comfort with the command line; start from the strfry or nostream README"), linking to the projects' own docs. If any setup detail survives, bump Node to a supported version.
**217. [src/content/guides/en/relay-guide.mdx:47] The guide is close to 95% bullet fragments; flowing prose exists only inside Callouts and one intro sentence, matching the July 2026 audit's bullet-soup pattern.**

- Evidence: Outside the four Callouts and the single sentence at line 268 ("Try before you commit! ..."), every section is scaffolded lists: "Why This Matters" (lines 47-65) is three bold headers with three fragments each; all three "Types of Relays" subsections repeat an identical What/Pros/Cons/Examples template (lines 94-174); "Selection Criteria" (lines 205-235) is five numbered headers with three fragments each; "Paid Relays > Why Pay?" (lines 351-366) and "Troubleshooting > Common Issues" (lines 490-539) are symptom/fix fragment pairs.
- Fix: Rewrite into short paragraphs: "Why This Matters" (one paragraph weaving the three benefits), the three "Types of Relays" intros (keep Examples as lists, fold What/Pros/Cons into 2-3 sentences each), "Selection Criteria" (one sentence of guidance per criterion instead of three fragments), and "Why Pay?". Keep genuinely list-shaped content as lists: the starter-set code block, the relay-count table, troubleshooting fix steps, and Quick Reference.
**218. [src/content/guides/en/relays-demystified.mdx:189] The nostr-rs-relay link points to a GitLab project that no longer exists (404).**

- Evidence: "[nostr-rs-relay](https://gitlab.com/soapbox-pub/nostr-rs-relay) - Rust implementation" (line 189). Verified 2026-09-01: GitLab API returns {"message":"404 Project Not Found"} for soapbox-pub%2Fnostr-rs-relay.
- Fix: Point to the maintained GitHub repo https://github.com/scsibug/nostr-rs-relay (verified active, last push 2026-05-22, not archived).
**219. [src/content/guides/en/relays-demystified.mdx:141] Paid relay fee claim '1000-5000 sats/month' understates real prices and assumes a subscription model that common paid relays don't use.**

- Evidence: "- Usually require small fee (often 1000-5000 sats/month)" (line 141). nostr.wine, the best-known paid relay (and one this very guide recommends), charges a one-time 18,888 sat fee; its filter service is 10,000 sats/month. The stated range matches neither the amount nor the billing model.
- Fix: Replace with a hedged, model-agnostic sentence: pricing varies by relay, typically a one-time admission fee or a subscription, from a few thousand up to roughly 20,000 sats. Or drop the numbers and say 'a small Bitcoin payment, one-time or monthly depending on the relay'.
**220. [src/content/guides/en/relays-demystified.mdx:184] Personal-relay benefits overpromise: 'Perfect reliability' and 'Privacy (only your data on the relay)' are both false framings.**

- Evidence: "- **Complete control** over your data\n- **Perfect reliability** (no dependency on others)\n- **Privacy** (only your data on the relay)" (lines 182-184). A personal relay is only as reliable as the owner's hosting; and it grants no privacy: notes are still broadcast publicly to other relays, and self-hosting ties the relay's IP to the user. For a creator (non-dev) audience this section also drifts into sysadmin territory.
- Fix: Rewrite the bullet list as: complete control over your data, a permanent personal backup of everything you post, independence from any single relay operator. Delete 'Perfect reliability' and the 'Privacy' bullet. Keep the pointer to /guides/relay-guide as the place for setup detail.
**221. [src/content/guides/en/relays-demystified.mdx:226] 'Consider a paid relay for permanent storage' promises permanence no relay can guarantee.**

- Evidence: "- Consider a paid relay for permanent storage" (line 226, under '\"My posts disappear\"'). Paid relays also prune, change policy, and shut down; nothing on Nostr guarantees permanence except redundancy.
- Fix: Change to: "Paid relays usually keep posts much longer, but nothing is guaranteed forever: post to several relays so no single one holds your only copy."
**222. [src/content/guides/en/relays-demystified.mdx:231] Troubleshooting tells beginners to open a wss:// URL in a browser, which browsers cannot navigate to.**

- Evidence: "- Try the relay URL in a browser (should show error/ws page)" (line 231). Relay URLs in this guide are all wss:// scheme; pasting one into a browser address bar either errors on the scheme or triggers a web search, and 'error/ws page' is meaningless to the target reader.
- Fix: Rewrite: "Swap wss:// for https:// in the relay address and open it in your browser: a live relay shows an info page or a short message, a dead one shows nothing." Or drop the step and keep only 'the relay might be down, try another one'.
**223. [src/content/guides/en/relays-demystified.mdx] Linked GitLab repo gitlab.com/soapbox-pub/nostr-rs-relay does not exist (404)**

- Evidence: WebFetch of https://gitlab.com/soapbox-pub/nostr-rs-relay returned HTTP 404. WebSearch confirms nostr-rs-relay's actual home is sourcehut (~gheartsfield/nostr-rs-relay), mirrored on GitHub as scsibug/nostr-rs-relay — it is not hosted under soapbox-pub on GitLab.
- Fix: Replace with the correct source: https://github.com/scsibug/nostr-rs-relay (GitHub mirror) or the sr.ht origin, since the soapbox-pub GitLab path is wrong.
**224. [src/content/guides/en/troubleshooting.mdx:411] Resources section links to wiki.nostr.com, a dead domain (NXDOMAIN)**

- Evidence: "- [Nostr Wiki](https://wiki.nostr.com)" — under 'Still Stuck?' > 'Resources', line 411. Verified 2026-09-01: DNS lookup returns NXDOMAIN from two independent resolvers (curl and WebFetch both fail with host-not-found).
- Fix: Replace with a live beginner resource, e.g. "- [Nostr.how](https://nostr.how)" or "- [nostr.com](https://nostr.com)" (both verified live).
**225. [src/content/guides/en/troubleshooting.mdx:401] Community-help links are broken or misleading: t.me/AmethystNostr does not exist, and t.me/nostr_protocol is a 364-subscriber broadcast channel where users cannot ask for help**

- Evidence: "- [Nostr Protocol](https://t.me/nostr_protocol) - General help" (line 399) and "- [Amethyst](https://t.me/AmethystNostr) - Android-specific" (line 401). Verified 2026-09-01: t.me/AmethystNostr returns Telegram's generic 'Contact @AmethystNostr' page (no such public group; Amethyst's README lists no Telegram at all), and t.me/nostr_protocol is a channel ('Preview channel', '364 subscribers'), i.e. broadcast-only, not a help group. t.me/damusios exists (146 members). Line 407's "Post with #help tag" also names the wrong hashtag; the ecosystem's help tag is #asknostr.
- Fix: Remove the nostr_protocol and AmethystNostr lines. Keep Damus Telegram, add "Amethyst: report issues on [GitHub](https://github.com/vitorpamplona/amethyst/issues)", and change line 407 to "Nostr itself - Post with the #asknostr tag".
**226. [src/content/guides/en/troubleshooting.mdx:137] Presents NIP-05 as a 'verified badge' that 'distinguishes real from fake', overstating what NIP-05 does; an impersonator can get their own NIP-05 on a free domain**

- Evidence: "**1. Get NIP-05 verified** ... **Why**: Verified badge distinguishes real from fake" — lines 133-137 under 'Problem: "Someone is Impersonating Me"'. NIP-05 itself says: "The NIP-05 is not intended to _verify_ a user, but only to _identify_ them". The trust signal is the specific domain, not the badge's presence.
- Fix: Rephrase the Why line: "**Why**: An address at a domain you control (you@yoursite.com) proves the account is tied to your site — followers check the domain, not just the checkmark, since anyone can get some NIP-05 address."
**227. [src/content/guides/en/troubleshooting.mdx:35] Suggests wss://nostr.wine as a beginner 'quick fix' relay without mentioning it is a paid, auth-gated relay (18,888 sats admission)**

- Evidence: "- **Quick fix**: Add popular relays: `wss://relay.damus.io`, `wss://nostr.wine`" — under 'Problem: "My Feed is Empty"', line 35. Verified 2026-09-01: nostr.wine's homepage calls itself "the most reliable paid relay on nostr" with a "One-time Admission Fee: 18,888 sats" and authentication on the relay. A new user adding it gets rejected writes and auth prompts, not a fixed feed.
- Fix: Replace nostr.wine with a free open relay: "`wss://relay.damus.io`, `wss://nos.lol`" (or `wss://relay.primal.net`; both verified live).
**228. [src/content/guides/en/troubleshooting.mdx:328] Claims zaps 'usually need 100+ sats for fees', which is invented; routing fees on small zaps are typically around a satoshi and tiny zaps work**

- Evidence: "- **Minimum**: Usually need 100+ sats for fees" — under 'Problem: "Zaps Not Working"', '2. Insufficient balance', line 328. Small zaps (1-21 sats) are routine on Nostr; Lightning routing fees for such payments are typically ~1 sat, and hosted-to-hosted transfers are often free.
- Fix: Replace with: "**Note**: Even tiny zaps work, but keep a few hundred sats in the wallet — a near-zero balance can make payments fail because of routing fees or wallet minimums."
**229. [src/content/guides/en/troubleshooting.mdx:285] The relay-down test 'Open relay URL in browser' fails as written: browsers cannot open wss:// URLs, and 'ws page' is unexplained jargon**

- Evidence: "- **Test**: Open relay URL in browser (should show error or ws page)" — under 'Problem: "Can't Connect to Relay"', '1. Relay is down', line 285. Typing a wss:// address into a browser triggers a search or an unknown-scheme error regardless of whether the relay is up, so the test tells the reader nothing.
- Fix: Change to: "**Test**: Change `wss://` to `https://` and open that address in your browser — a live relay usually shows a short info page or a 'use a Nostr client' message; no response at all means it's likely down."
**230. [src/content/guides/en/troubleshooting.mdx:249] Key-recovery step 'Check email for welcome messages' is fabricated: no Nostr client emails keys, and it primes users to trust phishing mail claiming to hold their nsec**

- Evidence: "- Check email for welcome messages" — under 'Problem: "Lost My Keys"', '1. Search for backups', line 249. Nostr signup involves no email anywhere in the flow; no client or signer sends keys by email.
- Fix: Delete the line and substitute realistic hiding places: "Check notes apps and cloud backups of old devices" and "Check the signer app (Amber, extension) on any device where you were logged in".
**231. [src/content/guides/en/troubleshooting.mdx:210] Crash fix 'Use NIP-65 outbox model' is unexplained protocol jargon a creator cannot act on, with no link to the site's own outbox guide**

- Evidence: "- **Optimize**: Use NIP-65 outbox model" — under 'Problem: "Client Crashes"', '2. Too many relays', line 210. The reader is a non-technical creator troubleshooting a crashing app; 'NIP-65 outbox model' names a spec, not an action, and the guide never links /guides/outbox-model.
- Fix: Change to: "**Optimize**: Let the app pick relays for you — see the [Outbox Model guide](/guides/outbox-model)" (slug exists in src/content/guides/en/).
**232. [src/content/guides/en/troubleshooting.mdx:35] Empty-feed quick fix tells beginners to add nostr.wine, a paid relay they cannot use.**

- Evidence: "**Quick fix**: Add popular relays: `wss://relay.damus.io`, `wss://nostr.wine`" ('My Feed is Empty', line 35). relay-guide.mdx labels nostr.wine paid; a user without the admission fee gets nothing from this 'quick fix'.
- Fix: Change to `wss://relay.damus.io`, `wss://nos.lol` — the free pair relay-guide and the FAQ's 'Essential' block already agree on.
**233. [src/content/guides/en/troubleshooting.mdx:411] "Nostr Wiki" link points to a non-existent domain (wiki.nostr.com)**

- Evidence: - [Nostr Wiki](https://wiki.nostr.com) — WebFetch: `getaddrinfo ENOTFOUND wiki.nostr.com`. Search turns up no such resource; real community wiki resources are at wikistr.com and nostrwiki.com.
- Fix: Replace with an actual live Nostr wiki, e.g. https://wikistr.com or https://www.nostrwiki.com, or remove the link.
**234. [src/content/guides/en/what-is-nostr.mdx:203] Quiz explanation claims relays "don't control who can post or what content is allowed", which is false for any individual relay and contradicts the very next quiz question.**

- Evidence: "Relays are servers that store your posts and make them available to other users. They forward messages but don't control who can post or what content is allowed." - explanation of the 'relay-function' question in guides.whatIsNostr.quiz.questions (src/i18n/locales/en.json), rendered by <WhatIsNostrQuiz client:idle /> at line 203 of the guide. The next question's explanation says the opposite: "If one relay blocks you, others can still carry your content." The guide body itself says "Some are free, some require payment".
- Fix: In src/i18n/locales/en.json, guides.whatIsNostr.quiz.questions, id 'relay-function', reword the explanation to: "Relays are servers that store your posts and make them available to other users. Each relay sets its own rules and can refuse content, but no relay controls the network: if one rejects you, you publish through others." Mirror the fix in the other locale files for this key.
**235. [src/content/guides/en/zaps-and-lightning.mdx:482] The "ZapSplits Tool" resource link is dead: github.com/nostr-protocol/zaps returns 404.**

- Evidence: Line 482: "- [ZapSplits Tool](https://github.com/nostr-protocol/zaps)". Verified 404 via fetch on 2026-09-01; no such repository exists in the nostr-protocol org.
- Fix: Delete the line. If a splits reference is wanted, link the NIP-57 spec (https://github.com/nostr-protocol/nips/blob/master/57.md) labeled as the technical spec, or drop it entirely since the guide targets non-developers.
**236. [src/content/guides/en/zaps-and-lightning.mdx:307] The privacy note has it backwards: zap amounts are always public in the receipt; what some clients can hide is the sender and message.**

- Evidence: Lines 307-308: "**Privacy note:** Zaps are semi-public. While amounts can be private in some setups, the fact that you zapped (or were zapped) is visible." NIP-57 requires the receipt's bolt11 invoiceAmount to equal the amount tag, so the amount is part of the public zap receipt; private/anonymous zaps (client extensions) hide the sender or message, not the amount.
- Fix: Replace with: "Privacy note: zaps are public. Anyone can see that a post was zapped and for how much. Some clients offer private or anonymous zaps that hide who sent the zap (and the message), but the amount on the post stays visible."
**237. [src/content/guides/en/zaps-and-lightning.mdx:297] Telling readers to verify zap receiving with "a testnet faucet" is a dead end: testnet coins cannot pay a mainnet Lightning address.**

- Evidence: Lines 295-297: "3. **Verify it works**\n   - Ask a friend to send you 10 sats\n   - Or use a testnet faucet". A testnet faucet dispenses testnet BTC, which cannot reach the mainnet Lightning address zaps use; a beginner following this cannot succeed and gets unexplained dev jargon on top.
- Fix: Delete the testnet faucet bullet. Replace with a workable self-check: zap one of your own posts from a second wallet, or send a few sats to your Lightning address from your wallet's send screen.
**238. [src/content/guides/en/zaps-and-lightning.mdx:358] The Zap Analytics tools are mischaracterized: zaplife.lol is a live zap feed, not "Detailed analytics", and nostrudel.ninja is a power-user client, not a stats tool.**

- Evidence: Lines 360-362: "- Most clients show basic stats\n- [zaplife.lol](https://zaplife.lol) - Detailed analytics\n- [nostrudel.ninja](https://nostrudel.ninja) - Advanced stats". Fetched zaplife.lol on 2026-09-01: it is a live zap firehose by Pablo F7z, not an analytics dashboard. noStrudel is a technical multi-purpose client aimed at power users, a poor fit for the site's creator audience, and "advanced stats" is not what it is for. The "For Supporters" bullets (zap leaderboard, impact tracking, lines 364-368) describe features no named tool provides.
- Fix: Trim the section to what is true: most clients show zap totals per post and a zap tab in notifications, and Primal's wallet screen shows your full send/receive history. Relabel zaplife.lol as "a live feed of zaps across Nostr, fun to watch" or drop it (also at line 481). Drop nostrudel.ninja and the invented "For Supporters" feature bullets.
**239. [src/content/guides/en/zaps-and-lightning.mdx:29] Most of the guide is bullet fragments rather than prose; the conceptual sections need rewriting into flowing paragraphs.**

- Evidence: Roughly 250 words of flowing prose in a ~1,700-word guide (the intro at lines 17-19, the Wallet of Satoshi passage at 162-167, and single sentences at 272 and 307); everything else is fragment bullets, e.g. "- Likes are free and worthless\n- Platform keeps all ad revenue\n- Creators get nothing\n- Engagement is hollow" (lines 33-36). This matches the July 2026 audit's ~90% bullet-fragment pattern.
- Fix: Rewrite these sections as paragraphs, keeping genuine step lists and tables as they are: "Why Zaps Are Revolutionary" (31-43), "Real Examples" (45-50), "How Zaps Appear" (69-76), "Custodial vs Non-Custodial" (142-158), "Zap Notifications" (299-308), "Common Use Cases" (339-344), "Zap Analytics" (348-368), "Creator Best Practices" (372-407), "Best Practices" (457-473), and "Community" (486-492). The Wallet of Satoshi passage (162-167) already shows the target register.
**240. [src/content/guides/en/zaps-and-lightning.mdx:25] Sat-to-dollar conversions imply a $30k Bitcoin, off ~2.6x from reality and inconsistent with the NIP-05 guide's pricing math.**

- Evidence: "They send satoshis → Tiny amounts of Bitcoin (1000 sats = ~$0.30)" (line 25) and "Developer receives 100,000 sats ($30) for fixing a bug" (line 47) imply BTC ≈ $30,000. nip05-identity.mdx's price table ("65,000 sats (~$40)", "12,500 sats (~$8)") implies BTC ≈ $62-64k. BTC is ~$78k as of 2026-09-01, so the zaps figures understate what readers would actually send/receive by ~2.6x — material for a guide telling creators what tips are worth.
- Fix: Recompute both zaps figures at a round ~$80k (1000 sats ≈ $0.80; 100,000 sats ≈ $80) or drop the USD equivalents and say "a fraction of a dollar"/"tens of dollars". Do the same pass on nip05-identity's USD column, or extend its existing 'Prices in USD are approximate' disclaimer to name the assumed BTC price so the two guides stop implying different ones.
**241. [src/content/guides/es/faq.mdx:544] False friend 'recipiente' used for 'recipient' (correct Spanish: 'destinatario')**

- Evidence: Line 544: "el recipiente está offline"; line 548: "Los relays del recipiente están offline o lentos"; line 726: "no el contenido o recipiente exacto". In Spanish 'recipiente' means a container/vessel. The nip17 guide and the FAQ's own zaps answer correctly use 'destinatario'/'receptor'.
- Fix: Replace 'recipiente' with 'destinatario' in lines 544, 548 and 726 (e.g. "el destinatario está offline", "Los relays del destinatario…", "ni el contenido ni el destinatario exacto").
**242. [src/content/guides/es/faq.mdx:35] FAQ uses 'llave pública/privada' throughout while the project glossary and the other three reviewed guides use 'clave'**

- Evidence: Line 35: "**npub** (llave pública) = Tu nombre de usuario"; 'llave' appears 28 times in faq.mdx vs 3 'clave'. docs/internal/CONTENT_TRANSLATION.md's Spanish glossary specifies "public key → clave pública / private key → clave privada (Standard)", and nip17-private-messages.mdx (24x), multi-client.mdx and nip05-identity.mdx all use 'clave'. nip05-identity.mdx line 379 also has two stray 'llave' ("nsec (llave privada)… npub (llave pública)").
- Fix: Normalize faq.mdx to 'clave/clave pública/clave privada' everywhere 'llave' refers to keys (28 occurrences), and fix the two 'llave' occurrences in es/nip05-identity.mdx line 379 to 'clave'.
**243. [src/content/guides/es/faq.mdx:79] Three cross-guide links keep English heading anchors that do not exist on the Spanish pages**

- Evidence: Line 79: "(/es/guides/keys-and-security#the-3-2-1-backup-rule)", line 103: "(/es/guides/keys-and-security#understanding-keys)", line 177: "(/es/guides/quickstart#choose-your-first-client)". The ES target headings are "## La Regla de Respaldo 3-2-1 (2 minutos)", "## Entendiendo las Claves (2 minutos)" and "## Paso 2: Elige Tu Cliente", so these fragments never match and the links land at the top of the page.
- Fix: Point the anchors at the slugs generated from the Spanish headings: #la-regla-de-respaldo-3-2-1-2-minutos, #entendiendo-las-claves-2-minutos and #paso-2-elige-tu-cliente (verify the exact IDs in the built pages). Note for the EN lane: the EN source anchors also omit the "(2 minutes)" suffix that the slugger includes, so en/faq.mdx likely has the same dead-anchor problem.
**244. [src/content/guides/es/finding-community.mdx:443] "No interactúes - Alimenta a los trolls" reads as an imperative to feed the trolls, inverting the EN meaning.**

- Evidence: Line 443: "4. **No interactúes** - Alimenta a los trolls" — EN line 443 is "**Don't engage** - Feeds trolls" (i.e. engaging feeds them); "Alimenta" is ambiguous between 3rd-person and the imperative "Feed the trolls".
- Fix: Change to "4. **No interactúes** - Responder alimenta a los trolls".
**245. [src/content/guides/es/multi-client.mdx:38] 'media' left in English where Spanish reads it as 'sock/average' — should be 'multimedia'**

- Evidence: Line 38: "- **Cliente A:** Excelente para media" and line 268: "**Mejor para media:**" (EN: "Great for media", "Best for media"). In Spanish 'media' is a stocking or an average, not media content.
- Fix: Line 38: "Excelente para multimedia"; line 268: "**Mejor para multimedia:**" (or "contenido multimedia").
**246. [src/content/guides/es/nip05-identity.mdx:477] 'Sigue leyendo' silently swaps the NIP-05 checker tool link for the nostr-tools guide and drops both link descriptions**

- Evidence: ES lines 477-478: "- [Inicio Rápido: Lánzate en 5 Minutos](/es/guides/quickstart)\n- [Herramientas Esenciales de Nostr](/es/guides/nostr-tools)" vs EN lines 478-479: "- [Check any NIP-05 address](/tools/nip05-checker) — verify a `name@domain` resolves to the key it claims\n- [Quickstart: launch in 5 minutes](/guides/quickstart) — if you do not have an account yet, start here". The checker link and both dash-explainers are gone.
- Fix: Restore both EN items with translated descriptions. The tools route is not localized (only src/pages/tools/nip05-checker.astro exists), so link the English route: "- [Comprueba cualquier dirección NIP-05](/tools/nip05-checker) — verifica que un `nombre@dominio` resuelve a la clave que dice ser\n- [Inicio rápido: lánzate en 5 minutos](/es/guides/quickstart) — si aún no tienes cuenta, empieza aquí". If sending ES readers to an English tool page is unwanted, keep the embedded checker reference but do not silently substitute a different guide.
**247. [src/content/guides/es/outbox-model.mdx:254] Stale "Sigue leyendo" section: EN's 2026-08-05 rewrite added one-line blurbs and new link labels that the ES file never received.**

- Evidence: ES lines 254-255 are bare links: "- [Cómo Viajan las Publicaciones: Relays Explicados](/es/guides/relays-demystified)". EN (commit 86cd625) reads: "- [Relays, demystified](/guides/relays-demystified) — what a relay actually is, before you tune which ones you use" and "- [Choosing and managing your relays](/guides/relay-guide) — how to pick a read and write set that makes the outbox model work for you".

- Evidence: "**Paso 4: Atardecer**" (line 267), translating EN "**Step 4: Sunset**" (en/privacy-security.mdx line 267). "Atardecer" only means dusk/sunset-the-scenery in Spanish; it has no "retire/phase out" sense.

Consulta nuestro [FAQ](/es/guides/faq) para más temas._" The glossary ships for es, so the dropped link points to real localized content.

**255. [src/content/guides/es/what-is-nostr.mdx:77] "no pueden detenerte de publicar" is an ungrammatical calque of "stop you from posting", used twice.**

**256. [src/content/guides/es/zaps-and-lightning.mdx:320] The verb for zapping flips between 'zapar', 'zapear' and bare English 'zap', including one outright ungrammatical sentence.**

- Evidence: Line 320: 'Cuando alguien zap tu publicación, divide automáticamente el pago' (no conjugated verb); line 241: '### Cómo Zapar una Publicación'; line 260: 'Zap lo que el contenido vale para ti'; line 461: '✅ Zap contenido que proporcione valor'; but lines 303/305/469 use 'te zapean' / 'te zapeó' / 'zapear'.
- Fix: Standardize on 'zapear' (the form the file already conjugates): line 241 '### Cómo Zapear una Publicación'; line 260 'Zapea lo que el contenido valga para ti'; line 320 'Cuando alguien zapea tu publicación, el pago se divide automáticamente entre tus colaboradores:'; line 461 '✅ Zapea contenido que aporte valor'.
**257. [src/content/guides/es/zaps-and-lightning.mdx:469] The 'No hagas:' list is rendered as bare subjunctive fragments that do not parse as Spanish.**

- Evidence: Lines 469-473 under '### No hagas:': '- ❌ Te sientas presionado a zapear', '- ❌ Zap más de lo que puedas permitirte', '- ❌ Esperes zaps a cambio', '- ❌ Hagas spam de contenido de baja calidad esperando zaps', '- ❌ Olvides respaldar tu seed de wallet' — each is a truncated calque of EN 'Don't: Feel pressured to zap' etc.; without a leading 'No' the subjunctives are ungrammatical.

**283. [src/content/guides/hi/troubleshooting.mdx:30] troubleshooting.mdx uses Latin 'Relay' throughout while the two relay guides it links to use Devanagari 'रिले', so the same concept switches script between adjacent guides.**

Relay कनेक्शन की समस्या**"), while hi/relay-guide.mdx (104) and hi/relays-demystified.mdx (66) use 'रिले' exclusively. Repo-wide, 10 of 16 hi guides use 'रिले' (~345 hits) vs 4 using Latin (~86). LESSONS_HI_LOCALE.md nominally says protocol terms stay in English, which the corpus contradicts.
- Fix: Pick one form and apply it everywhere. Practical direction: convert troubleshooting.mdx (and, outside this task's four guides, faq.mdx and finding-community.mdx) to 'रिले' to match the dominant form and the two relay-focused guides, then update the terminology note in docs/internal/LESSONS_HI_LOCALE.md to record 'रिले' as the sanctioned Hindi rendering (npub/nsec/NIP-xx stay Latin).

This ties the tables and steps back to the Custodial vs Non-Custodial section.
**317. [src/content/guides/zh/faq.mdx:78] Three cross-guide links keep English heading anchors that do not exist on the zh pages, so they land at the top of the page instead of the section.**

- Evidence: Line 78: "(/zh/guides/keys-and-security#the-3-2-1-backup-rule)"; line 102: "(/zh/guides/keys-and-security#understanding-keys)"; line 176: "(/zh/guides/quickstart#choose-your-first-client)". Built HTML ids on those zh pages are Chinese slugs: dist/zh/guides/keys-and-security/index.html has id="3-2-1-备份规则2-分钟" (and "理解密钥（2 分钟）" → "理解密钥2-分钟"); dist/zh/guides/quickstart/index.html has id="步骤-2选择你的客户端". scripts/check-links.mjs strips fragments (line 38: href.split('#')[0]), so this is never caught.
- Fix: Point the anchors at the generated zh ids: #3-2-1-备份规则2-分钟, #理解密钥2-分钟, #步骤-2选择你的客户端 (zh/nip17-private-messages.mdx already localises its anchors this way, e.g. #投递私信中继列表kind-10050).
**318. [src/content/guides/zh/faq.mdx:721] Garbled sentence in the DM-privacy answer: a stray 使用 turns "relay operators can see..." into "using relay operators...".**

- Evidence: Line 721: "使用中继操作员可以看到你在发消息，但使用 NIP-17 看不到内容或确切的接收者。" EN: "Relay operators can see you're messaging but not content or exact recipient with NIP-17."
- Fix: Rewrite as: "中继运营者能看到你在发消息，但在 NIP-17 下看不到内容，也看不到确切的接收者。"
**319. [src/content/guides/zh/multi-client.mdx:195] Broken conditional clause: "如果在分心，在桌面版上禁用" is not grammatical Chinese.**

- Evidence: Line 195: "- 如果在分心，在桌面版上禁用". EN: "Disable on desktop if distracting".
- Fix: Rewrite as: "- 如果桌面通知让你分心，就把它关掉".
**320. [src/content/guides/zh/nip05-identity.mdx:99] "强烈推荐" is left as an orphan line after the bullet list — the EN sentence "it's highly recommended if you're:" was split apart and reads broken.**

- Evidence: Lines 91–99: "**但是...** 如果你正在：\n\n- 建立个人品牌\n...- 厌倦阅读 63 个字符的字符串\n\n强烈推荐". EN: "**But...** it's highly recommended if you're: [list]". The zh list item also drops "long-term" ("Serious about Nostr long-term" → "认真对待 Nostr").
- Fix: Restructure to: "**但是...** 如果你符合以下情况，强烈推荐：" followed by the list (change one item to "打算长期认真使用 Nostr"), and delete the orphan "强烈推荐" line.
**321. [src/content/guides/zh/nostr-tools.mdx:159] 自我托管 used for both "self-custodial" (wallets) and "self-host" (servers), and sits confusingly next to 托管/非托管 meaning "custodial".**

- Evidence: Line 159 (Primal Wallet): "基于 Spark 的自我托管" (EN: "Self-custodial (Spark-based)"); line 173 (Phoenix): "最适合：想要自我托管的移动用户" directly above line 174 "功能：非托管" — the reader sees 'wants 自我托管' praised while 非托管 (non-custodial) is listed as the feature; line 320 (table): "移动自我托管". Meanwhile line 148 uses the same word for server self-hosting: "想要自我托管的技术用户". 托管 alone means custodial (line 164 Coinos: "托管式网页钱包"), so 自我托管 for self-custody is ambiguous.
- Fix: Use 自主保管 for self-custody of funds/keys: line 159 → "自主保管（基于 Spark）"; line 166 Alby Hub parenthetical → "（自主保管的闪电节点）"; line 173 → "想要自主保管资金的移动用户"; table line 320 → "移动端自主保管". Keep 托管/非托管 for custodial/non-custodial, and for server self-hosting (line 148) use 自建 or 自行架设: "想要自建中继的技术用户". Same fix applies to privacy-security.mdx:230 "从托管转向自托管" → "从托管钱包转向自主保管".
**322. [src/content/guides/zh/outbox-model.mdx:25] "Relay" rendered as 继电器 (electrical relay component) in one sentence, while the rest of the guide uses 中继.**

- Evidence: "你会发布到你最喜欢的继电器，但你的关注者可能使用完全不同的继电器。" (under "旧方式（Outbox 模型之前）", line 25). 继电器 means an electrical relay switch; every other occurrence in this guide and all other zh guides uses 中继 (verified via grep: this is the only 继电器 in src/content/guides/zh/).
- Fix: Replace both occurrences: "你会把帖子发布到你最喜欢的中继，但你的关注者可能使用完全不同的中继。"
**323. [src/content/guides/zh/outbox-model.mdx:2] Guide uses "Outbox 模型" while the prerequisite guide, troubleshooting guide, and this page's own quiz (zh.json) all use 发件箱模型 for the same concept.**

- Evidence: Frontmatter: title: "理解 Outbox 模型" and body headings like "## 什么是 Outbox 模型？" (lines 2, 16, 27, 40, 42, 46, 52, 132, 192). But src/content/guides/zh/relays-demystified.mdx:157 introduces the term as 称为"发件箱模型", zh/troubleshooting.mdx:209 uses 发件箱模型, and src/i18n/locales/zh.json:1579 titles this very guide's card "理解发件箱模型" with the embedded quiz using 发件箱模型 throughout. A reader clicks a card named 理解发件箱模型, lands on a page titled 理解 Outbox 模型, and the quiz at the bottom switches back to 发件箱模型.
- Fix: Standardize on 发件箱模型 (already established by the prerequisite guide and the JSON card/quiz): set frontmatter title to "理解发件箱模型", replace "Outbox 模型"/"outbox 模型" with 发件箱模型 throughout the body, keeping one English gloss on first definition at line 42: "发件箱模型（Outbox Model，NIP-65）". Leave the English "outbox"/"inbox" inside the jsonc code block and the (outbox)/(inbox) marker glosses at lines 77-78 as-is.
**324. [src/content/guides/zh/outbox-model.mdx:29] "advertising where you post" mistranslated using 广告 (the noun 'advertisement') as a verb, producing broken Chinese in the key solution sentence.**

- Evidence: "Outbox 模型通过**广告你在哪里发布**来解决这个问题。" (under "解决方案：Outbox 模型", line 29). 广告 is not a verb in Chinese; this is a machine-translation calque of "by advertising where you post".
- Fix: Rewrite as: "发件箱模型通过**公开宣告你在哪里发帖**来解决这个问题。"
**325. [src/content/guides/zh/outbox-model.mdx:238] Section heading "Spread the Word" translated literally as 传播这个词 ("spread this vocabulary word"), which is nonsensical in Chinese.**

- Evidence: "### 传播这个词" (Take Action section, line 238). EN heading is "Spread the Word", an idiom meaning "tell others".
- Fix: Replace the heading with "### 让更多人知道" (the bullets beneath it already fit this reading).
**326. [src/content/guides/zh/privacy-security.mdx:260] Untranslated English remnant in a step heading: "第 4 步： sunset" with a stray space.**

- Evidence: "**第 4 步： sunset**" (How to Rotate section, line 260). EN source: "Step 4: Sunset". Steps 1-3 are fully translated (准备/宣布/迁移); this heading was left half in English, with a leading space after the colon.
- Fix: Replace with "**第 4 步：逐步告别旧账户**" (and remove the stray space).
**327. [src/content/guides/zh/privacy-security.mdx:428] "Compromised" consistently rendered as 泄露 ("leaked"), which reads as nonsense when applied to accounts and to Nostr itself.**

- Evidence: Line 428: "**带外通信** - 如果 Nostr 被泄露，如何联系关注者？" (EN: "if Nostr is compromised") — "Nostr got leaked" is meaningless. Line 439 (communication template): "我的账户 [旧 npub] 已被泄露。" (EN: "has been compromised"). Also lines 404, 411, 415, 418, 434. 泄露 is correct only for exposed keys/data; for a compromised account or platform the term is 被入侵 / 被盗用.
- Fix: Keep 泄露 where EN means exposure of the key itself (e.g. 私钥意外暴露), but change account/platform compromise wording: line 428 → "如果 Nostr 本身被入侵，如何联系关注者？"; line 439 → "我的账户 [旧 npub] 已被盗用。"; line 404 → "如果我的账户被盗用，我会告诉谁？"; line 411 heading → "### 如果你怀疑账户被盗用"; line 415 → "**停止从被盗用的账户发帖**"; line 434 → "如果账户被盗用，发布类似这样的内容：".
**328. [src/content/guides/zh/quickstart.mdx:250] The 'Explore All Guides' card links to the English guides index instead of the Chinese one.**

- Evidence: Line 250: 'href="/guides"' inside the '继续学习' (Keep Learning) card. /guides is the English guides index served by src/pages/[...lang]/guides/index.astro; the localized route /zh/guides exists. The other internal guide link in this file is correctly prefixed ('/zh/guides/keys-and-security', line 38). Note: the '/follow-pack' link on line 231 is correct as-is — that page is English-only (src/pages/follow-pack.astro), no /zh/ version exists.
- Fix: Change line 250 to href="/zh/guides". (Same bug exists in pl/quickstart.mdx line 257 — worth a note to the caller, outside this zh scope.)
**329. [src/content/guides/zh/relays-demystified.mdx:99] Section lead-in is an ungrammatical calque of 'helps when you', with 'troubleshoot' used as a transitive verb in the bullet below.**

- Evidence: "但理解这一点有助于当你：" (line 99) and bullet "需要故障排除缺失的帖子" (line 103)
- Fix: Replace lines 99-103 with: "大多数 Nostr 客户端会默认帮你连上几个中继。但在下面这些情况下，理解这一点会很有用：" and change the third bullet to "需要排查帖子缺失的问题".
**330. [src/content/guides/zh/relays-demystified.mdx:159] The 'Instead of / You do' pair is translated word-for-word and reads as broken Chinese.**

- Evidence: "**代替**：你手动连接到 10+ 个中继\n**你做**：发布一次你的首选中继列表，让客户端弄清楚" (lines 159-160)
- Fix: Rewrite as: "**过去的做法**：手动连接 10 多个中继\n**现在只需**：发布一次你的首选中继列表，剩下的交给客户端处理".
**331. [src/content/guides/zh/relays-demystified.mdx:205] Three of the four Best Practices bullets keep English word order (trailing adverbial clauses), which a native reader stumbles on.**

- Evidence: "**包含一个\"写入\"中继** 你总是发布到那里" (line 206), "**检查中继正常运行时间** 在依赖它之前" (line 207), "**保持你的中继列表更新** 随着网络增长" (line 208)
- Fix: Reorder into natural Chinese: line 206 "**固定一个\"写入\"中继**，每次发帖都发到那里"; line 207 "**在依赖某个中继之前，先查看它的正常运行时间**"; line 208 "**随着网络发展，及时更新你的中继列表**".
**332. [src/content/guides/zh/relays-demystified.mdx:135] 'Spam' on relays is translated as 垃圾邮件 (junk email) instead of 垃圾信息, contradicting relay-guide.mdx in the same locale.**

- Evidence: "可能充满垃圾邮件" (line 135) and "更少垃圾邮件" (line 142); relay-guide.mdx line 208 correctly uses "有些会过滤垃圾信息"
- Fix: Change both occurrences to 垃圾信息 ("可能充斥垃圾信息", "垃圾信息更少") to match relay-guide.mdx; 垃圾邮件 specifically means unsolicited email.
**333. [src/content/guides/zh/relays-demystified.mdx:149] NIP-65 outbox model has two different Chinese names across zh guides: 发件箱模型 here (and in troubleshooting.mdx and zh.json) vs 'Outbox 模型' throughout zh/outbox-model.mdx.**

- Evidence: "## 高级：发件箱模型（NIP-65）[高级]" (line 149) and troubleshooting.mdx line 209 "使用 NIP-65 发件箱模型", while outbox-model.mdx line 2 is title: "理解 Outbox 模型" and uses "Outbox 模型" throughout despite its zh.json card saying "理解发件箱模型"
- Fix: Standardize on 发件箱模型: update zh/outbox-model.mdx to use 发件箱模型 with "（Outbox 模型）" as a one-time gloss at first mention, matching zh.json and the two guides audited here. No change needed in this file itself beyond optionally adding the same one-time English gloss.
**334. [src/content/guides/zh/troubleshooting.mdx:155] Feed spam section uses 垃圾邮件 (junk email) for spam posts/accounts, wrong for a social feed context.**

- Evidence: "## 问题：\"动态中有垃圾邮件\" 🗑️" (line 155) and "**1. 关注了垃圾邮件账号**" (line 165)
- Fix: Use 垃圾信息/垃圾内容: heading "动态里全是垃圾信息", bullet "关注了发垃圾信息的账号"; also line 178 "添加垃圾发送者" reads better as "把发垃圾信息的账号加进去".
**335. [src/content/guides/zh/zaps-and-lightning.mdx:272] Garbled sentence: 'This personalizes the interaction' rendered as "这个性化了互动", which parses as nonsense (这 + 个性化 fused).**

- Evidence: "这个性化了互动，帮助创作者知道什么内容有共鸣。" (line 272)
- Fix: Replace with: "这会让互动更有人情味，也能帮助创作者了解哪些内容引起了共鸣。"
**336. [src/content/guides/zh/zaps-and-lightning.mdx:16] The guide never glosses 闪电打赏 as 'zap', while the surrounding UI (card title, quiz, embedded simulator) uses the English word Zap, leaving readers unable to connect the two.**

- Evidence: Guide uses only 闪电打赏 (first mention line 16), but zh.json has guides.zapsAndLightning.title = "Zaps 与闪电网络", zapSimulator.title = "Zap 模拟器", troubleshootingWizard "Zap 按钮无反应"; the guide also says "使用 #zap 标签讨论" (line 490) without ever introducing the word zap.
- Fix: On first mention (line 16) write "闪电打赏（zap）是通过闪电网络发送的比特币小费" and add the gloss once in troubleshooting.mdx line 308 area too; this makes the English UI labels, the #zap hashtag, and English client buttons recognizable. Keep 闪电打赏 elsewhere.
**337. [src/content/guides/zh/zaps-and-lightning.mdx:22] 'Thread' translated as 线程 (a programming term) three times, wrong register for the creator audience.**

- Evidence: "一条帖子、线程或回复" (line 22), "好的帖子/线程" (line 256), "很棒的线程，学到了很多" (line 268)
- Fix: Use 帖子串 (or 长帖): "一条帖子、帖子串或回复", "优质帖子/帖子串", "这个帖子串很棒，学到了很多". The same 线程 calque also appears in zh/faq.mdx:452, zh/privacy-security.mdx:350-351, zh/multi-client.mdx:180, zh/finding-community.mdx:282,312 and should be fixed site-wide in a follow-up.
**338. [src/data/glossary/en.ts:37] Client examples list Iris, which is no longer a current beginner recommendation in September 2026, and omit Primal.**

- Evidence: "Examples: Damus, Iris, Amethyst." (en.ts line 37, 'client' entry; same trio in pl.ts:37, es.ts:37, de.ts:37). Iris pulled its mobile apps from stores in 2024 and survives as a niche web client; 2026 roundups (e.g. Soapbox's "Best Nostr Apps in 2026") recommend Primal as the default cross-platform starter alongside Damus (iOS) and Amethyst (Android). The site already features Primal elsewhere (src/i18n/locales/en.json, src/data/relay-browsing-clients.ts).
- Fix: In all four glossary files change the example list to 'Primal, Damus, Amethyst' (client names stay untranslated). Note for a separate pass outside the glossary: the same stale Iris mention exists in src/components/interactive/ClientRecommender.tsx:120, src/pages/resources.astro:65, and the 'Damus, Amethyst, Iris' string in src/i18n/locales/*.json.
**339. [src/data/glossary/en.ts:70] User-visible term labels '_nsec' and '_npub' read as a rendering bug and sort to the top of the glossary in every locale.**

- Evidence: term: '_nsec' (en.ts line 70) and term: '_npub' (en.ts line 74), repeated in pl.ts/es.ts/de.ts lines 70/74. glossary.astro renders item.term verbatim as the visible <h3> (line 111), feeds it into JSON-LD DefinedTerm names (line 60), and sorts by localeCompare (line 48), so underscore-prefixed pseudo-terms lead the page and the structured data in all four locales. The index.ts comment only reserves the merge-vs-keep decision for ptrio42; the underscore label itself is an artifact of the old page, not part of that decision.
- Fix: Without merging the entries (that decision stays open), rename the displayed labels in each locale file: en 'nsec (format)' / 'npub (format)', pl 'nsec (format)' / 'npub (format)', es 'nsec (formato)' / 'npub (formato)', de 'nsec (Format)' / 'npub (Format)'. This fixes the page heading, the JSON-LD term name, and the sort position in one edit per file.
**340. [src/i18n/locales/ar.json:901] "Custodial" mistranslated as "ائتمانية" (credit-related) in the zaps and key-security quizzes, contradicting the correct "وصائية" used in the same guide**

- Evidence: guides.zapsAndLightning.quiz.questions[4].prompt (line 901): "ما الفرق بين محافظ Lightning الائتمانية والمحافظ ذاتية الحفظ؟"; options at lines 905/913 and explanation at 917 repeat "الائتمانية". Also guides.keysAndSecurity.quiz.questions[5].options[0].label (line 377): "إعادة استخدام نفس المحفظة الائتمانية لكل هوية" and its explanation (line 389): "تجنب مشاركة حسابات الائتمان بين الهويات" (reads as "credit accounts"). The same file translates custodial correctly two questions earlier: line 873 "وصائية مثل Wallet of Satoshi".
- Fix: Replace "الائتمانية/الائتماني" with "الوصائية" in lines 377, 901, 905, 913, 917, and change line 389 to "وتجنب مشاركة المحافظ الوصائية بين الهويات". "ائتماني" means credit-related in Arabic and misnames the custody concept; the marked answers still work only because the parentheticals define the term.
**341. [src/i18n/locales/ar.json:962] multiClient data-sync question (severity: critical) was rewritten, losing the "no central sync server" teaching point and clashing with Q4 of the same quiz**

- Evidence: guides.multiClient.quiz.questions[1]: EN correct option "Via relays - all clients read from and write to the same relay network" became "استخدام نفس Relays على كل العملاء" (line 962); EN distractor "Through a central sync server" was dropped and replaced with "نسخ ولصق يدوي" (line 958) plus new distractor "لا يمكن مزامنتها" (line 966); the explanation (line 969) became an instruction: "استخدم نفس Relays على كل العملاء...". Q4 (relay-consistency) then says using the same relays is "يُوصى به للاتساق، لكنه غير إلزامي", which now reads as contradicting Q1's correct answer.
- Fix: Retranslate faithfully: a = "عبر خادم مزامنة مركزي", b = "عبر Relays - جميع العملاء يقرؤون ويكتبون على نفس شبكة Relays", c = "تحتاج إلى نسخ البيانات يدوياً بين الأجهزة", explanation = "Relays هي طبقة المزامنة: عندما تنشر من عميل واحد يذهب المنشور إلى Relays، وتقرأ بقية العملاء من نفس Relays لعرض محتواك."
**342. [src/i18n/locales/ar.json:2190] Client recommender results-screen header translated as an intro tagline, so the results page tells the user to go get a recommendation they are already viewing**

- Evidence: clientRecommender.title (line 2189): "موصي العملاء" vs EN "Your Perfect Client"; clientRecommender.description (line 2190): "احصل على توصية عميل Nostr مناسب لك." vs EN "Based on your preferences, here are our top recommendations". ClientRecommender.tsx renders both only inside the `if (showResults ...)` branch (lines 362-365), i.e. above the computed recommendations.
- Fix: Change title to "عميلك المثالي" and description to "بناءً على تفضيلاتك، هذه أفضل توصياتنا".
**343. [src/i18n/locales/ar.json:2769] Backup checklist description drops the "critical - lost keys cannot be recovered" warning**

- Evidence: backupChecklist.description (line 2769): "تأكد من أن مفاتيحك آمنة." vs EN "Before proceeding, complete this checklist to ensure your keys are safely backed up. This is critical - lost keys cannot be recovered." (The irreversibility warning survives lower down in copiedNsec.warning and the skip modal, so the tool is not unsafe, but the header framing is lost.)
- Fix: Replace with "قبل المتابعة، أكمل هذه القائمة للتأكد من نسخ مفاتيحك احتياطياً بأمان. هذا أمر بالغ الأهمية - المفاتيح المفقودة لا يمكن استردادها." Optionally align the title with EN: "انسخ مفاتيحك احتياطياً" instead of "قائمة مراجعة النسخ الاحتياطي".
**344. [src/i18n/locales/de.json:722] False friend 'tippen' garbles the zap explanation: German readers read 'to type', not 'to tip'.**

- Evidence: guides.zapsAndLightning.quiz.questions[0].explanation (id: what-are-zaps): "Sie ermöglichen es dir, Content-Ersteller sofort mit echtem Geld zu tippen oder zu unterstützen." English source: "They allow you to tip or support content creators instantly with real money." The guide's own German title correctly uses 'Trinkgelder' for tips.
